const SCALE = 6.0;        // millivolts at a release probability of one
function run(spikeTimes, duration) {
  let u = U0, epsp = 0, ca = 0, retro = 0;
  const amps = [];
  for (let t = 0; t < duration; t += DT) {
    u -= ((u - U0) / TAU_F) * DT;
    epsp -= (epsp / TAU_E) * DT;
    ca -= (ca / TAU_CA) * DT;
    retro -= (retro / TAU_RETRO) * DT;
    if (spikeTimes.some(s => Math.abs(t - s) < DT / 2)) {
      const amp = SCALE * u * (1 - 0.8 * retro);  // facilitating, then retro-gated
      epsp += amp;
      u += DF * (1 - u);
      amps.push(Number(amp.toFixed(2)));
    }
    if (epsp > VTH) { ca += 1; epsp = 0; }        // the bitufted cell fires
    if (ca > 2) { retro = 1; ca = 0; }            // dendritic GABA travels backwards
  }
  return amps;
}
const burst = [];
for (let k = 0; k < 12; k++) burst.push(50 + k * 25);  // a 40 hertz pyramidal burst
console.log("input amplitudes in millivolts:", run(burst, 600).join(", "));

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Two vertical dendritic tufts spanning the whole cortical depth, an input that gets stronger with every pyramidal spike while the neighbouring multipolar cell's input gets weaker, and the rare ability to release GABA backwards out of its own dendrite.

CHAPTER 36. HORIZONTAL CELLS OF LAYER 1 (Next-Column Tuft-Quietening Neuron) (COG)

At the very roof of your brain, in a skin of tissue almost empty of cell bodies, a few ancient neurons lie flat on their sides like surveyors who pegged out the site and never left.

The old label is half a shape word and half an address, combining a spatial orientation with a layer number without teaching purpose.

Next-Column names the reach that makes this cell unusual: its axon runs sideways rather than up and down, possessing the longest horizontal collaterals of any cell in the top sheet and carrying its influence past its own column into adjacent ones.

Tuft-Quietening names the target and effect: the top sheet is where the apical tufts of pyramidal cells gather to receive feedback, and inhibition delivered here determines whether those incoming expectations can influence the cell body.

Neuron marks it as a specialized upper-layer interneuron, enabling one patch of cortex to quieten the incoming feedback of neighboring columns.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

This is a neuron whose body and dendrites lie parallel to the brain's surface instead of pointing into it.

It lives in layer 1, also called the molecular layer, the outermost of the six sheets of the neocortex.

The famous members are the Cajal-Retzius cells, among the first neurons born in the human cortex, appearing five to seven weeks after conception.

Their main output is chemical, not electrical: they secrete reelin, a giant protein of 3461 amino acids and about 388 kilodaltons, which tells later-born neurons where the top of the cortex is.

Because of reelin the cortex builds itself inside out, each new wave climbing past the older cells and stopping just below the surface.

In adults a smaller population persists and works as a slow horizontal binder, spreading excitation sideways across the dendritic tufts of many different columns.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The cell body is a spindle, tapered at both ends, lying with its long axis parallel to the pia mater, the thin membrane wrapping the brain.

One or two long, sparsely branched dendrites run out horizontally from the poles.

Unlike almost every other cortical neuron there is no apical dendrite reaching up and no skirt of basal dendrites reaching down.

The axon runs tangentially in a flat plexus that stays inside layer 1.

SIZE:

The soma is roughly 10 to 30 micrometres along its long axis and 6 to 12 micrometres across.

Horizontal dendrites commonly reach 300 to 900 micrometres, which is remarkable for a cell this small.

The tangential axon plexus can span one to two millimetres, crossing several cortical columns.

Layer 1 itself is only about 0.2 to 0.3 millimetres thick, roughly a tenth of the cortical thickness.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Three shapes are described in adult human cortex: horizontal, triangular and multipolar, and their numbers do not fall with age.

Developmentally there are two waves, transient Cajal-Retzius neurons that vanish and persisting ones that appear around 30 weeks of gestation and stay for life.

The persisting cells express reelin, the transcription factor Tbr1, p73, calretinin, the chemokine receptor CXCR4 and nitric oxide synthase.

A related calretinin-positive form in primate prefrontal layer 1 is called the subpial fan cell.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The address is neocortical layer 1, specifically its upper part, pressed against the glia limitans.

The glia limitans is the felt of astrocyte end-feet forming the brain's outer wall, so this neuron sits at the exact interface between brain tissue and the cerebrospinal fluid of the subarachnoid space.

They occur in every neocortical area, and equivalents sit in the hippocampal molecular layer near the hippocampal fissure, the outer molecular layer of the dentate gyrus, layer 1 of entorhinal cortex, the subiculum and layer 1 of piriform cortex.

The second interface that matters is the layer 1 to layer 2 boundary, where their axons meet apical tufts arriving from below.

NUMBER:

Layer 1 as a whole holds roughly 150 to 250 million neurons across both hemispheres, about one to one and a half percent of the 16.34 billion neocortical neurons.

That comes from layer 1 being about a tenth of cortical thickness with a measured density in human primary visual cortex of 11,900 neurons per cubic millimetre against a whole-cortex mean of 79,200.

Horizontal cells are a small minority of that.

My best estimate is 3 million, range 1 to 8 million, and confidence is low because nobody has counted them directly in whole human brains.

That is about 0.02 percent of neocortical neurons and about 0.003 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons in the nervous system.

The three types counted in this batch are horizontal cells at 3 million, the Far-Hushing Slow-Wave Neuron at 25 million and the Cortex-To-Cortex Linking Neuron at 200 million, some 228 million in total, about 1.4 percent of the regional budget.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Most measurements come from developing rodent cortex, so treat them as indicative.

Input resistance is high, 300 to 800 megaohms, so a tiny current makes a large voltage swing.

Resting potential sits depolarised at about minus 60 to minus 50 millivolts, close to firing.

A hyperpolarisation-activated current, written Ih, produces a slow rebound sag whenever the cell is pushed down, giving it a springy, self-righting feel.

Spikes are broad, adaptation is strong, and firing rates stay under about 10 per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs arrive from the thalamic matrix, the diffuse calbindin-positive projection that spreads across layer 1 rather than targeting one column.

More arrive from cortico-cortical feedback axons running horizontally in layer 1, from cholinergic fibres of the nucleus basalis of Meynert, from serotonergic dorsal raphe fibres and from noradrenergic locus coeruleus fibres.

Outputs go onto the apical tufts of pyramidal neurons in layers 2, 3 and 5, and onto other layer 1 interneurons.

In development these cells are coupled to each other by gap junctions, protein channels that let current pass directly between cells.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Thin, under half a micrometre, unmyelinated, running tangentially for one to two millimetres.

Conduction is slow, roughly 0.1 to 0.5 metres per second, so a signal needs several milliseconds to cross one cortical area.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

The fast transmitter is glutamate, the brain's main excitatory chemical.

The signature product is reelin, released into the extracellular space rather than a synaptic cleft.

Reelin binds apolipoprotein E receptor 2 and the very low density lipoprotein receptor, which then phosphorylate an inner adaptor called Disabled-1 through Src family kinases.

Some cells also make nitric oxide, a gas that diffuses freely and widens nearby blood vessels.

Calretinin, used to stain these cells, is not a transmitter but a calcium-binding buffer.

MYELINATION:

Essentially none, and layer 1 is the least myelinated layer of the cortex.

Bare axons are one reason conduction here is so slow.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Electrically cheap, because the cell fires rarely and drives few synapses.

Chemically expensive, because building and exporting a 388 kilodalton glycoprotein loads the endoplasmic reticulum and Golgi apparatus, the cell's protein factory and packing depot.

Expect an unusually high ratio of manufacturing cost to spiking cost.

PLASTICITY:

Reelin does not stop working when development ends.

In adults it strengthens the N-methyl-D-aspartate receptor, a glutamate receptor that acts as a coincidence detector, and it supports long-term potentiation, the lasting strengthening of synapses that underlies memory.

It also controls the maturation of dendritic spines, the small knobs where excitatory synapses land.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The glia here are genuinely unusual and have no parallel deeper in the cortex.

Layer 1 holds interlaminar astrocytes, found only in primates, with a soma about 10 micrometres across and one or two tortuous processes diving up to a millimetre through layers 2 to 4.

Glia limitans end-feet separate these neurons from cerebrospinal fluid by a single cellular sheet, so they are unusually exposed to whatever that fluid carries.

Microglia, the brain's resident immune cells, clear most Cajal-Retzius cells by programmed cell death in early life, so microglial restraint is a condition of this cell's survival.

Oligodendrocyte support is minimal because there is almost nothing here to myelinate.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Take these cells out during development and the cortex inverts: neurons pile up in the wrong order and the six-sheet architecture collapses into a disordered slab.

Take them out of the adult and layer 1 loses its horizontal binder, the sideways excitation that lets tufts from widely separated columns be nudged together.

The region would keep its columns and lose its cross-column glue, along with the standing reelin tone that keeps distal dendrites willing to change.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The main output is not a spike but a protein poured into the extracellular space, a pure case of volume transmission where the message reaches whoever holds the receptor.

Reelin is then cut by extracellular proteases of the ADAMTS family, so its range and lifetime are set outside the cell that made it.

Gap junctions let a population depolarise together in development.

Immature cells release glutamate from dendrites as well as axons, so the whole surface can be an output surface.

Nitric oxide synthase lets them widen local capillaries, coupling their own activity to blood flow.

Sitting against the cerebrospinal fluid and carrying CXCR4, they read chemical gradients in that fluid as a positional signal.

Housekeeping is dominated by the secretory pathway, and failure of secretion, not failure of firing, is the plausible way this cell breaks.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: glutamatergic drive from thalamic matrix and cortico-cortical feedback axons in layer 1, 0 to 200 picoamps; neuromodulation by acetylcholine, serotonin and noradrenaline as a gain term of 0.8 to 1.5; ambient gamma-aminobutyric acid, the brain's main inhibitory chemical, at 0.1 to 1.0 micromolar.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate onto apical tufts of pyramidal neurons in layers 2, 3 and 5 within about 1 millimetre; reelin as a scalar concentration field in layer 1; nitric oxide as a vasodilator field over about 100 micrometres.

• STATE: membrane voltage; sag activation variable; intracellular calcium; reelin pool; recent spike count.

• PARAMETERS: input resistance 300 to 800 megaohms; resting potential minus 60 to minus 50 millivolts; threshold about minus 42 millivolts; maximum sag conductance about 4 nanosiemens.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 to 30 milliseconds; sag activation about 180 milliseconds; reelin clearance about 2 minutes; developmental reelin gradient, weeks.

• FAILURE MODE: reelin messenger RNA and protein fall by roughly half in the cortex of people with schizophrenia, alongside hypermethylation of the RELN promoter, a chemical tag that silences a gene; altered Cajal-Retzius density is used to classify focal cortical dysplasia, a malformation causing drug-resistant epilepsy; total loss of reelin signalling gives lissencephaly, a smooth brain with grossly disordered layers.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to decide how much reelin a horizontal cell of layer one releases beneath the pia, given the input arriving along its long flat dendrites.
Create a number variable called membrane voltage with initial value negative sixty five.
Create a number variable called sag current with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called reelin pool with initial value one.
Create a number variable called spike count with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called step counter with initial value zero.
While step counter is less than eight thousand, repeat the following steps.
    Create a number variable called input current with initial value sixty.
    Calculate the difference of membrane voltage and negative eighty two.
    Set sag current to the quotient of that difference divided by negative two hundred.
    Calculate the sum of input current and sag current.
    Set membrane voltage to the sum of membrane voltage and the quotient of that sum divided by fifty.
    Check if membrane voltage is greater than or equal to negative forty two.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set membrane voltage to negative fifty eight.
        Set spike count to the sum of spike count and one.
        Set reelin pool to the sum of reelin pool and two hundredths.
    End the conditional block.
    Set reelin pool to the product of reelin pool and nine hundred and ninety nine thousandths.
    Set step counter to the sum of step counter and one.
End the loop.
Display spike count to the console.
Display reelin pool to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the behaviour of interest is a slow secretory process sitting on top of slow spiking, so clarity beats speed and no numerical library is needed.

# Python model of a layer 1 horizontal cell: slow spiking plus reelin release.
import math

DT = 0.5                                   # time step, milliseconds
E_LEAK, R_IN, TAU_M = -65.0, 400.0, 25.0   # mV, megaohms, ms (leaky and slow)
V_THRESH, V_RESET = -42.0, -58.0           # mV
E_H, G_H_MAX, TAU_H = -30.0, 0.004, 180.0  # sag current: mV, microsiemens, ms
TAU_REELIN = 120000.0                      # reelin pool empties over ~2 minutes

def step(v, h, reelin, i_pA):
    """Advance one 0.5 ms step. i_pA is tuft input in picoamps."""
    h_inf = 1.0 / (1.0 + math.exp((v + 82.0) / 7.0))   # sag opens when hyperpolarised
    h += DT * (h_inf - h) / TAU_H
    i_h = G_H_MAX * h * (v - E_H) * 1000.0             # microsiemens x mV = nA -> pA
    v += DT * (-(v - E_LEAK) - (i_h - i_pA) * R_IN / 1000.0) / TAU_M
    spike = v >= V_THRESH
    if spike:
        v = V_RESET
    # Reelin is made slowly, dumped under the pia on each spike, cleared slowly,
    # so the cell reports its recent history rather than this instant.
    reelin += DT * (-reelin / TAU_REELIN) + (0.02 if spike else 0.0)
    return v, h, reelin, spike

v, h, reelin, count = -65.0, 0.0, 1.0, 0
for k in range(int(4000 / DT)):                        # simulate 4 seconds
    drive = 60.0 if 1000.0 <= k * DT < 3000.0 else 0.0 # 60 pA tuft input for 2 s
    v, h, reelin, s = step(v, h, reelin, drive)
    count += 1 if s else 0
print("spikes in 4 s: %d   reelin pool: %.3f   resting Vm: %.1f mV" % (count, reelin, v))

Run, it prints 30 spikes in 4 seconds, a reelin pool of 1.557 and a resting potential of minus 62.2 millivolts.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only cortical neuron that lies down.

Find a spindle soma flat against the pia, long horizontal dendrites, no apical dendrite, calretinin inside and reelin pouring out, and you have found a horizontal cell of layer 1.


PART THREE: HIPPOCAMPAL FORMATION - MEMORY AND NAVIGATION

CHAPTER 37. DENTATE GRANULE CELLS (COG)

Fifteen million tight-lipped little cells stand at the front door of your memory system, and almost all of them stay silent almost all of the time.

Dentate names their home: the tooth-like, serrated layer of the hippocampus where these cells sit packed side by side.

Granule Cell describes their shape and size: tiny, densely packed grains of neurons whose sparse firing creates distinct, non-overlapping codes for every memory entering the brain.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The dentate granule cell is a small excitatory neuron, a nerve cell that makes its targets fire, and its job is pattern separation: giving two nearly identical experiences two very different codes.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

One fan of dendrites, the branches that collect incoming signals, rises from a small oval cell body, with none below it in the healthy adult.

Dendritic length totals 2,800 to 3,500 micrometres at 1.3 to 1.6 spines per micrometre, giving 3,600 to 5,600 excitatory contacts.

SIZE:

The cell body is about 10 micrometres across, a seventh the width of a human hair, and the dendritic fan reaches 300 to 400 micrometres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Suprapyramidal cells of the upper blade are larger and spinier than infrapyramidal cells below, and adult-born cells spend weeks in an immature stage far more excitable than their neighbours.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies pack four to eight deep in the granule cell layer of the dentate gyrus, dendrites occupy the inner, middle and outer molecular layer, and axons cross the hilus into stratum lucidum of CA3.

The defining interface is the subgranular zone, where granule cell layer meets hilus and new granule cells are born.

NUMBER:

Unbiased stereology, a counting method that avoids guessing from thin slices, gives about 15 million per hemisphere and 30 million in total, roughly 0.035 percent of the nervous system's 86 billion neurons.

Confidence is good, with published values of 9 to 17 million per side.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage is unusually low at minus 75 to minus 85 millivolts, input resistance is only 100 to 300 megaohms, the membrane time constant is 20 to 40 milliseconds, and most cells never fire at all.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

The perforant path from entorhinal layer two brings objects and smells to the outer molecular layer and space to the middle, mossy cells supply the inner molecular layer, and each granule cell reaches only 11 to 18 CA3 pyramidal cells.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The mossy fibre axon is 0.2 micrometres thick and 3 to 5 millimetres long, bearing a giant bouton 4 to 10 micrometres wide with up to 35 release sites every 150 micrometres, plus thin branches onto ten times more interneurons.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate, the standard excitatory messenger, is packaged with free zinc by the transporter ZnT3, and the same terminals release the opioid dynorphin.

MYELINATION:

Mossy fibres carry no fatty insulating sheath, so conduction is slow at 0.2 to 0.5 metres per second.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

A cell that almost never fires has among the lowest running costs of any principal neuron, and most of that goes to mitochondria inside its terminals.

PLASTICITY:

Strengthening the perforant path needs dendritic spikes and the NMDA receptor, a glutamate receptor that opens only in an already depolarised cell, while the mossy fibre strengthens presynaptically without the target's help.

This layer may also add roughly 700 new neurons a day, a disputed figure.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The subgranular zone stem cells are astrocytes that kept the power to divide, astrocytes wrap the giant boutons incompletely so glutamate deliberately spills, and microglia eat newborn cells that fail.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete granule cells and the dentate gyrus becomes a plain wire: entorhinal traffic pours into CA3 undiluted, similar days collide, and the region loses its zinc and its new neurons.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Dendrites fire their own sodium and calcium spikes, without which the perforant path cannot be strengthened.

Zinc diffuses from the terminal as volume transmission, acting on a neighbourhood rather than a partner, and newborn cells are excited rather than inhibited by GABA because their chloride is high.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: 4,000 perforant path synapses from entorhinal layer two at 0 to 40 hertz, mossy cell input, inhibition to 60 hertz.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate and zinc onto 11 to 18 CA3 pyramidal cells, ten times as many interneurons, 7 to 12 mossy cells.

• STATE: membrane voltage, adaptation current, synaptic weights, terminal facilitation, cell age.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 80 millivolts, threshold minus 45 millivolts, resistance 200 megaohms, sparseness 1 to 3 percent.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 to 40 milliseconds, facilitation 100 milliseconds to seconds, maturation 4 to 8 weeks.

• FAILURE MODE: in epilepsy the axons sprout onto other granule cells and build a self-exciting loop; in Alzheimer's disease the input layer thins.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to fire only the most strongly driven granule cells, so that similar inputs produce different codes.
Create a number variable called firing_threshold with initial value -45.
Create a number variable called allowed_winners with initial value 20.
Create a list variable called cell_drives with initial value empty list.
Create a list variable called winners with initial value empty list.
Create a number variable called drive with initial value 0.
For each cell in the population, perform the following operations.
    Set drive to -80.
    For each weight in the active inputs of cell, perform the following operations.
        Calculate the sum of drive and weight.
        Set drive to the result.
    End the loop.
    Add drive to the end of cell_drives.
End the loop.
Set winners to the strongest allowed_winners entries of cell_drives.
For each winner in winners, perform the following operations.
    Check if the drive of winner is greater than firing_threshold.
    Display the result to the console.
End the loop.
Return winners to the calling function.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because pattern separation is a population sorting problem and Python states the sparseness rule in one line.

import random
random.seed(7)
N_CELLS, N_INPUTS, K = 1000, 400, 20     # only 20 cells in 1000 may fire
W = [[random.random() if random.random() < 0.05 else 0.0    # sparse weights
      for _ in range(N_INPUTS)] for _ in range(N_CELLS)]
def drive(active):                       # summed excitation for every cell
    return [sum(row[j] for j in active) for row in W]
def winners(d):                          # inhibition as k-winners-take-all
    return set(sorted(range(len(d)), key=lambda i: d[i], reverse=True)[:K])
def overlap(a, b):
    return len(a & b) / float(min(len(a), len(b)))
one = set(random.sample(range(N_INPUTS), 40))
two = set(list(one)[:36]) | set(random.sample(           # 90 percent the same
    [i for i in range(N_INPUTS) if i not in one], 4))
print("input  overlap %.2f" % overlap(one, two))
print("output overlap %.2f" % overlap(winners(drive(sorted(one))),
                                      winners(drive(sorted(two)))))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A small oval body in a densely packed layer, one fan of dendrites and none below, a resting voltage near minus 80 millivolts, near-total silence, and an axon studded with giant zinc-filled boutons.

CHAPTER 38. SEMILUNAR GRANULE CELLS (Signal-Sustaining Bridge Neuron) (COG)

Just above the granule cell layer sits a crescent-shaped cousin that answers a brief flash of input by firing for the next ten seconds.

Semilunar means half-moon in Latin, describing the crescent spread of the cell's dendrites in the inner molecular layer - an anatomical description that tells a student nothing about function.

Signal-Sustaining names the cell's defining property: a brief burst of input throws these cells into a long plateau depolarization, keeping them firing for up to ten seconds after the input stops.

Bridge names the purpose that sustained firing serves: carrying an event forward across a gap in time so that experiences separated by seconds can be bound into a single memory.

Neuron marks it as a glutamatergic principal cell rather than an interneuron.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The semilunar granule cell is an excitatory neuron of the dentate gyrus that looks like a granule cell but works as a memory buffer, latching into a plateau potential, a long sustained depolarisation, and firing on after the world has moved on.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The cell body is wider than tall, giving the half-moon shape that names it, two to four thick dendrites spread over 300 micrometres across the molecular layer, and the axon is a mossy fibre to CA3 with collaterals that stay behind.

SIZE:

The cell body is 10 to 14 micrometres across, and the dendritic span is one and a half to two times a granule cell's.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

No agreed subtypes exist, and some laboratories treat semilunar and ordinary granule cells as one continuum, so use a single type with a graded plateau-strength parameter.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The cell bodies sit in the inner molecular layer of the dentate gyrus and the top edge of the granule cell layer, and that border is the interface defining the cell.

Dendrites reach the middle and outer molecular layer while the axon reaches CA3.

NUMBER:

In the mouse these cells are at most 3 percent of granule cells, and under 5 percent of dentate projection neurons.

Scaling from 15 million granule cells per hemisphere gives an upper bound near 750,000 per side, under 0.002 percent of the nervous system, at low confidence.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Given a long depolarising step the cell fires throughout instead of falling silent, and between spikes the voltage climbs in a slow ramp carried by voltage-gated calcium channels, pores that open on depolarisation and admit calcium.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Excitation arrives by the perforant path, and the outputs matter most: these cells are the main source of excitation onto the cell bodies of parvalbumin basket cells, the fast inhibitory neurons that enforce sparseness, and they drive mossy cells and hilar interneurons into barrages lasting up to ten seconds.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The main axon is a mossy fibre with giant boutons, but the distinguishing feature is a set of collaterals that never leave the dentate gyrus and run through the inner molecular layer.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the transmitter, and as a mossy fibre the axon is expected to carry vesicular zinc, with no peptide signature yet established.

MYELINATION:

The axon carries no insulating sheath, so conduction is slow and local.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Plateaus are costly, because every calcium ion admitted must be pumped out against a ten-thousand-fold gradient using adenosine triphosphate, the cell's energy currency.

PLASTICITY:

Synaptic plasticity here is poorly characterised and that should be said plainly, but persistent firing binds neurons into transient cell assemblies, storage needing no synaptic change.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The glia are ordinary inner molecular layer astrocytes with no known specialisation, though they must clear the potassium released during long plateaus, and failure there would stretch plateaus toward the pathological.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete this cell and the dentate gyrus loses its short-term memory: a brief input gives a brief answer, the hilar barrages vanish, and basket cells lose their main drive.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The plateau is dendritic computation in its purest form, a regenerative calcium event turning a millisecond input into a multi-second output.

Because it excites basket cells on their somata it is a feed-forward inhibition generator, and its collaterals allow recurrent excitation where none is usually described.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: perforant path onto wide dendrites, local excitation from mossy cells, dendritic inhibition that ends plateaus.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate onto parvalbumin basket cell somata, mossy cells, hilar interneurons, CA3 pyramidal cells.

• STATE: membrane voltage, plateau flag, plateau time remaining, calcium, spike count.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 70 to minus 75 millivolts, threshold minus 45 millivolts, plateau 20 to 30 millivolts lasting 300 to 3,000 milliseconds.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 25 milliseconds, plateau decay 300 to 3,000 milliseconds, barrage up to 10 seconds.

• FAILURE MODE: runaway plateaus could seed seizures, while losing the cell removes short-term binding and weakens feed-forward inhibition.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to make one semilunar granule cell keep firing long after a brief input has ended.
Create a number variable called membrane_voltage with initial value -70.
Create a number variable called firing_threshold with initial value -45.
Create a number variable called plateau_drive with initial value 27.
Create a number variable called plateau_remaining with initial value 1200.
Create a number variable called elapsed_time with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called total_drive with initial value 0.
While plateau_remaining is greater than 0, repeat the following steps.
    Set total_drive to plateau_drive.
    Check if elapsed_time is less than 50.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the sum of total_drive and 40.
        Set total_drive to the result.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the difference of plateau_remaining and 0.5.
    Set plateau_remaining to the result.
    Calculate the sum of -70 and total_drive.
    Calculate the difference of the result and membrane_voltage.
    Calculate the quotient of the result divided by 50.
    Calculate the sum of membrane_voltage and the result.
    Set membrane_voltage to the result.
    Check if membrane_voltage is greater than firing_threshold.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set membrane_voltage to -55.
        Display elapsed_time to the console.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the sum of elapsed_time and 0.5.
    Set elapsed_time to the result.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the plateau is a small state machine and the contrast fits in two printed lines.

DT, TAU_M = 0.5, 25.0                      # step and membrane time constant, ms
V_REST, V_THRESH, V_RESET = -70.0, -45.0, -55.0
PLATEAU_DRIVE, PLATEAU_MS = 27.0, 1200.0   # calcium plateau, millivolts and ms
def run(on, stim_ms=50.0, total_ms=2000.0):
    v, left, spikes = V_REST, 0.0, []
    for step in range(int(total_ms / DT)):
        t = step * DT
        drive = 40.0 if t < stim_ms else 0.0    # perforant path stimulus
        if on and drive > 0.0 and left <= 0.0 and v > -60.0:
            left = PLATEAU_MS                   # brief input latches plateau
        if left > 0.0:
            drive, left = drive + PLATEAU_DRIVE, left - DT
        v += ((V_REST + drive) - v) / TAU_M * DT
        if v >= V_THRESH:
            spikes.append(t)
            v = V_RESET
    return spikes
for on in (False, True):
    s = run(on)
    print("%s: %d spikes, last at %.1f ms"
          % ("plateau on" if on else "plateau off", len(s), s[-1] if s else 0.0))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A wide, flat, crescent cell body above the granule layer, dendrites fanning unusually wide, and a response to a brief shock that will not stop.

CHAPTER 39. MOSSY CELLS (COG)

Cajal drew them covered in moss: they are the busiest listeners in the hippocampus, and they are the first cells to die when the temporal lobe turns epileptic.

Mossy is a plain-English shape word describing the thorny excrescences that cover the cell's proximal dendrites like moss on a tree branch - a vivid visual image that makes the cell instantly recognizable under a microscope.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The mossy cell is a large excitatory neuron of the hilus, the hollow interior of the V-shaped dentate gyrus, and it broadcasts local granule cell activity to distant slices of the same structure, usually as net inhibition.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Three to five thick dendrites leave a large multipolar cell body, and their first 100 micrometres are studded with thorny excrescences, multi-headed spines that look like clumps of moss and catch giant mossy fibre boutons.

SIZE:

The cell body is 25 to 35 micrometres across, three times a granule cell's, and dendrites extend 300 to 500 micrometres in the hilus.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Ventral cells stain strongly for the calcium-binding protein calretinin and dorsal cells faintly, and a minority send one dendrite out through the granule cell layer.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Mossy cells occupy the hilus of the dentate gyrus, also called the polymorphic layer or CA4, bounded by the two granule cell blades and the CA3c border.

Their axons run for millimetres along the long axis to the inner molecular layer on the same side and, far more sparsely in humans, to the opposite hemisphere.

NUMBER:

No human count exists: the rat has about 30,000, and stereology gives roughly 2 million hilar neurons per human hippocampus, of which mossy cells are the major excitatory share.

A working figure is 0.5 to 1.5 million per hemisphere, near 0.002 percent of the nervous system, at low confidence.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Mossy cells receive spontaneous excitatory events at a far higher rate than any other dentate cell, hundreds per second, yet fire sparsely because their threshold climbs after every spike.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs are granule cell mossy fibres onto thorny excrescences, back-projections from CA3, hilar interneurons, and fibres from the medial septum and the supramammillary nucleus.

Outputs reach granule cell dendrites and interneurons in the inner molecular layer.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The axon is the associational and commissural pathway of the dentate gyrus: thin, heavily branched, millimetres long, and studded with small boutons rather than giant ones.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the transmitter, and these cells also release sonic hedgehog, a signalling protein of embryonic development that here keeps the local stem cell pool alive.

MYELINATION:

The axons are thin and largely unmyelinated, so news crosses the hippocampus in tens of milliseconds.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Handling hundreds of excitatory events per second is brutally expensive, and the pumps restoring the ionic gradients run near capacity.

PLASTICITY:

Both the granule cell to mossy cell synapse and the return synapse support long-term potentiation, and the cell governs structural plasticity by supplying sonic hedgehog to newborn neurons.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Hilar astrocytes carry an unusually heavy glutamate clearance load, and when they fail the mossy cells die first, the leading explanation for this cell's notorious fragility.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove mossy cells and the dentate gyrus becomes a stack of disconnected slices: activity here no longer quietens there, and the structure drifts toward hyperexcitability.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The climbing threshold is a genuine computation, turning a saturating input into a sparse output with no inhibition required.

Sonic hedgehog release makes the cell a developmental regulator, and detonation means one presynaptic spike can produce one postsynaptic spike.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: 30 to 50 giant mossy fibre synapses at 0 to 40 hertz, CA3 back-projection, septal modulation, spontaneous events at 100 to 500 hertz.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate onto granule cell dendrites and interneurons across 2 to 6 millimetres of the long axis.

• STATE: membrane voltage, adaptive threshold, calcium load, spike count.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 65 millivolts, threshold minus 45 millivolts, threshold step 4 to 8 millivolts, event amplitude 1 to 3 millivolts.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 15 to 25 milliseconds, threshold recovery 200 to 400 milliseconds, long-axis conduction 10 to 40 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE: selective death in temporal lobe epilepsy and after head injury, which removes long-range feedback inhibition and leaves granule cells prone to synchronous discharge.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn a dense barrage of excitatory events into a sparse spike train using a threshold that rises with every spike.
Create a number variable called membrane_voltage with initial value -65.
Create a number variable called spike_threshold with initial value -45.
Create a number variable called threshold_step with initial value 6.
Create a number variable called event_amplitude with initial value 2.2.
Create a number variable called elapsed_time with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called output_count with initial value 0.
Create a boolean variable called event_arrived with initial value false.
While elapsed_time is less than 20000, repeat the following steps.
    Set event_arrived to the outcome of a fair coin toss.
    If event_arrived is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the sum of membrane_voltage and event_amplitude.
        Set membrane_voltage to the result.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the difference of -65 and membrane_voltage.
    Calculate the quotient of the result divided by 20.
    Calculate the sum of membrane_voltage and the result.
    Set membrane_voltage to the result.
    Calculate the difference of -45 and spike_threshold.
    Calculate the quotient of the result divided by 250.
    Calculate the sum of spike_threshold and the result.
    Set spike_threshold to the result.
    Check if membrane_voltage is greater than spike_threshold.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set membrane_voltage to -65.
        Calculate the sum of spike_threshold and threshold_step.
        Set spike_threshold to the result.
        Calculate the sum of output_count and 1.
        Set output_count to the result.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the sum of elapsed_time and 1.
    Set elapsed_time to the result.
End the loop.
Display output_count to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the point is the ratio between a huge input count and a tiny output count.

import random
random.seed(3)
V_REST, THETA_BASE, THETA_STEP = -65.0, -45.0, 6.0
TAU_M, TAU_THETA, EPSP = 20.0, 250.0, 2.2    # milliseconds and millivolts
v, theta, n_in, n_out = V_REST, THETA_BASE, 0, 0
for step in range(20000):                    # twenty seconds, one ms per step
    if random.random() < 0.5:                # about 500 events per second
        n_in += 1
        v += EPSP
    v += (V_REST - v) / TAU_M                # leak back toward rest
    theta += (THETA_BASE - theta) / TAU_THETA    # threshold recovers slowly
    if v >= theta:
        n_out += 1
        v, theta = V_REST, theta + THETA_STEP    # adaptation after each spike
print("input events %d, output spikes %d" % (n_in, n_out))
print("%.0f inputs per output spike" % (n_in / float(max(n_out, 1))))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A big multipolar cell in the hollow of the dentate gyrus, proximal dendrites shaggy with thorny excrescences, drowning in excitation yet firing rarely, with an axon that runs millimetres to another slice of its own structure.

CHAPTER 40. CA3 PYRAMIDAL CELLS (Whole-From-Fragment Recall Neuron) (COG)

This is the cell that lets you recall a whole evening from the smell of one dish, because its axons loop back onto its own neighbours.

CA3 (Cornu Ammonis 3) is a historical Latin address on a map of the ram's horn, telling a newcomer nothing about function.

Whole-From-Fragment names the classic computation known as pattern completion, in which a partial cue is expanded back into the entire stored memory.

Recall names the faculty, driven by the dense recurrent web these cells make with one another - where roughly thirty to seventy percent of a CA3 cell's output synapses land on fellow CA3 cells - so the memory is literally rebuilt by mutual re-excitation.

Neuron marks it as a principal excitatory projection cell of the hippocampal circuit.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The CA3 pyramidal cell is a large excitatory neuron whose axons connect massively to its own neighbours, making the region an autoassociative memory that rebuilds a whole pattern from a fragment.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A classic pyramid sends one thick apical dendrite up and several basal dendrites down, and the first stretch of that apical dendrite carries thorny excrescences which catch giant mossy fibre boutons.

SIZE:

The cell body is 20 to 30 micrometres across and the apical dendrite reaches 500 to 800 micrometres, with 10 to 16 millimetres of dendrite in total in the rat.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Anatomists divide CA3 into strips CA3c, CA3b and CA3a by distance from the dentate gyrus, and a sharper split separates thorny cells from athorny cells, which lack excrescences and burst readily.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies fill stratum pyramidale of CA3, with dendrites in stratum oriens below and strata lucidum, radiatum and lacunosum-moleculare above.

The two interfaces are the hilar border, where CA3c sits inside dentate territory, and the CA3a to CA2 border, where mossy fibre input stops.

NUMBER:

About 3 million per hemisphere and 6 million in total, roughly 0.007 percent of the nervous system, or one neuron in 14,000.

Human stereology gives about 2.7 million for CA2 and CA3 counted together, so treat the figure as accurate within about 30 percent.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Many CA3 cells are intrinsic bursters, firing complex spikes of two to six action potentials driven by calcium channels and stopped by calcium-activated potassium channels, from a resting voltage near minus 68 millivolts.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Three excitatory inputs converge: about 50 giant mossy fibre boutons from granule cells, thousands of weak recurrent collaterals from other CA3 cells, and a direct perforant path from entorhinal layer two.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

One axon branches immediately into associational collaterals back into CA3, Schaffer collaterals forward to CA1, back-projections into the hilus, and a long branch to the lateral septum.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the transmitter, and acetylcholine from the septum suppresses recurrent transmission during encoding and releases it during recall.

MYELINATION:

Local collaterals are bare while the long fibres in the alveus and fimbria are myelinated, conducting at several metres per second.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Bursting costs several times more than single spiking and tens of thousands of synapses must be maintained, so CA3 is dense with mitochondria and intolerant of any break in glucose or oxygen.

PLASTICITY:

Recurrent synapses use NMDA receptor dependent potentiation, which writes a pattern in one trial, while the mossy fibre synapse strengthens presynaptically without that receptor, so teaching and storage obey different rules.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytes in stratum lucidum wrap the giant boutons only partly, permitting deliberate glutamate spillover, which is the opposite of what astrocytes are built to do everywhere else.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove CA3 and the hippocampus loses one-shot memory, recall from partial cues, and the sharp wave ripples that replay experience during rest and sleep.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

A mossy fibre input can unblock NMDA receptors at nearby recurrent synapses so that a subthreshold pattern becomes a supralinear response.

CA3 axons are also joined by gap junctions, direct electrical pores that help generate very fast ripples, and dense packing allows ephaptic nudging with no synapse at all.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: 50 mossy fibre synapses at 0 to 40 hertz, 6,000 to 12,000 recurrent synapses, 4,000 perforant path synapses, inhibition to 100 hertz.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate onto other CA3 cells, onto CA1 by Schaffer collaterals, onto hilar mossy cells, and to the lateral septum.

• STATE: membrane voltage, burst counter, calcium, recurrent weight matrix, after-hyperpolarisation current.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 68 millivolts, threshold minus 50 millivolts, burst 2 to 6 spikes, recurrent connection probability 2 to 5 percent.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 to 30 milliseconds, burst interval 5 to 15 milliseconds, after-hyperpolarisation 100 to 500 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE: excess recurrent gain gives seizures, since CA3 is the most epileptogenic tissue in the brain, while too little gives failure of recall from partial cues.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to rebuild a stored pattern from a fragment, using recurrent connections and a few clamped mossy fibre inputs.
Create a list variable called state with initial value empty list.
Create a list variable called clamped_units with initial value empty list.
Create a number variable called running_sum with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called unit_position with initial value 0.
Repeat the following steps exactly 6 times.
    Set unit_position to 0.
    For each unit in state, perform the following operations.
        Get the total number of items in clamped_units.
        Check if unit_position is less than the result.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Calculate the sum of unit_position and 1.
            Set unit_position to the result.
            Skip to the next iteration of the loop.
        End the conditional block.
        Calculate the sum of unit_position and 1.
        Set unit_position to the result.
        Set running_sum to 0.
        For each partner in state, perform the following operations.
            Calculate the product of the weight joining unit and partner and the value of partner.
            Calculate the sum of running_sum and the result.
            Set running_sum to the result.
        End the loop.
        Check if running_sum is greater than or equal to 0.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set the value of unit to 1.
        Otherwise, perform the following actions.
            Set the value of unit to -1.
        End the conditional block.
    End the loop.
End the loop.
Display the number of units matching the target pattern to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the recurrent network is a matrix sweep and Python keeps the storage rule and the recall rule to one line each.

import random
random.seed(1)
N, SWEEPS = 64, 6
pats = [[random.choice((1, -1)) for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(2)]
W = [[0] * N for _ in range(N)]
for p in pats:                              # Hebbian storage on recurrent collaterals
    for i in range(N):
        for j in range(N):
            if i != j: W[i][j] += p[i] * p[j]
s = [pats[0][i] if i < 20 else -1 for i in range(N)]      # degraded fragment
for sweep in range(SWEEPS):
    for i in range(N):
        if i < 4: s[i] = pats[0][i]; continue    # mossy fibre clamp, detonation
        s[i] = 1 if sum(W[i][j] * s[j] for j in range(N)) >= 0 else -1
print("recovered %d of %d bits"
      % (sum(1 for i in range(N) if s[i] == pats[0][i]), N))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A large pyramid whose proximal apical dendrite is knobbly with thorny excrescences, firing in bursts, and wired more densely to its own neighbours than any other cell type in the human brain.

CHAPTER 41. CA2 PYRAMIDAL CELLS (Familiar-Individual Recognition Neuron) (COG)

A strip of tissue half a millimetre wide, ignored for a century, turns out to be where the brain files the identities of the individuals you know.

CA2 (Cornu Ammonis 2) is another historical map coordinate - a narrow strip between two larger neighbours - that hides the most striking functional fact about the region.

Familiar-Individual names what the cell keeps track of: silencing these cells abolishes an animal's memory for specific individuals it has met before while leaving general sociability, spatial memory, and contextual memory completely intact.

Recognition names the faculty: the direct sense of having met this specific one before, distinct from recalling where or when that meeting occurred.

Neuron marks it as an excitatory principal cell whose output reaches the lateral septum and hypothalamus - the primary route by which social memory triggers social behavior.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The CA2 pyramidal cell is a large excitatory neuron in the narrow band between CA3 and CA1, and it is the hub for social memory, the recognition of an individual you have met before.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

It is a large pyramid with few or no thorny excrescences, and its tuft in stratum lacunosum-moleculare receives an entorhinal input strong enough to fire the cell on its own.

Many cell bodies are wrapped in a perineuronal net, a lattice of sugar-coated proteins that cages the cell.

SIZE:

The cell body is 20 to 30 micrometres across, and the region is a ribbon about 500 micrometres wide but some 30 millimetres long in humans.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Cells split by depth into deep and superficial, and by how strongly they carry the marker proteins Purkinje cell protein 4 and regulator of G protein signalling 14.

WHERE IT LIVES:

CA2 occupies stratum pyramidale between CA3a and CA1, with dendrites in stratum oriens, radiatum and lacunosum-moleculare.

Both borders are true interfaces, one where mossy fibre input ends and one where the marker proteins fade, and the cell also receives fibres from the hypothalamic paraventricular and supramammillary nuclei.

NUMBER:

No reliable stand-alone human count exists, because stereology pools CA2 with CA3.

From that pooled 2.7 million and CA2's small width, 200,000 to 350,000 per hemisphere is defensible, about 0.0006 percent of the nervous system, at low confidence.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

CA2 cells are harder to excite than their neighbours, resting near minus 70 millivolts with low input resistance, firing regularly rather than in bursts, and are most active during immobility and slow-wave sleep.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Schaffer collaterals from CA3 arrive in stratum radiatum, a very strong entorhinal layer two input arrives at the tuft, and the main output is a powerful projection to deep CA1 that bypasses the classical circuit.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The axon runs through stratum oriens and the alveus and arborises widely in deep CA1, so a few CA2 cells influence a great many CA1 cells.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the transmitter, but the cell is defined by what it listens to: the brain's highest density of vasopressin 1b receptors, plus abundant oxytocin receptors.

MYELINATION:

Output axons in the alveus are myelinated for fast delivery to CA1 and beyond, while local collaterals are largely bare.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Running costs are moderate, but the cell invests heavily in calcium pumps and buffering proteins, an investment repaid as survival through ischaemia, seizures and trauma that kill its neighbours.

PLASTICITY:

Ordinary potentiation at the Schaffer collateral input is blocked by regulator of G protein signalling 14, which limits calcium in the spines, and by the perineuronal net, and the gate opens only when vasopressin or oxytocin permits.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The perineuronal net here is the clearest case in the brain of glia and neurons jointly building a structure that limits learning, and it is unique in surrounding an excitatory cell rather than an inhibitory one.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove CA2 and the hippocampus keeps places but loses people, the shortcut to deep CA1 vanishes, and the region forfeits its one island of resistance to seizure and stroke.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The perineuronal net is memory stored outside the cell, a structural brake on synaptic change.

Vasopressin and oxytocin reach CA2 partly by volume transmission, diffusing through tissue rather than crossing a synapse, so the learning rule is set by the chemistry of the surrounding fluid.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: Schaffer collaterals into stratum radiatum, strong entorhinal layer two input at the tuft, vasopressin and oxytocin at 0 to 100 nanomolar, dense inhibition.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate onto deep CA1 across a wide span, back onto CA3, and to the lateral septum.

• STATE: membrane voltage, spine calcium, plasticity gate, perineuronal net integrity, accumulated potentiation.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 70 millivolts, threshold minus 48 millivolts, calcium clamp about 0.25, vasopressin gain about 4.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 15 to 25 milliseconds, calcium extrusion 20 to 100 milliseconds, peptide action seconds to minutes, net turnover days.

• FAILURE MODE: net degradation in schizophrenia, autism and Alzheimer's disease produces loss of social recognition while spatial memory survives.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to decide whether a CA2 synapse may strengthen, given repeated pairings and the presence or absence of vasopressin.
Create a number variable called synaptic_weight with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called calcium_total with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called clamp_fraction with initial value 0.25.
Create a number variable called potentiation_threshold with initial value 3.
Create a number variable called learning_rate with initial value 0.1.
Create a number variable called calcium_this_pairing with initial value 0.
Create a boolean variable called vasopressin_present with initial value false.
If vasopressin_present is true, then perform the following actions.
    Set clamp_fraction to 1.
End the conditional block.
Repeat the following steps exactly 10 times.
    Set calcium_this_pairing to clamp_fraction.
    Calculate the sum of calcium_total and calcium_this_pairing.
    Set calcium_total to the result.
    Check if calcium_total is greater than potentiation_threshold.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of learning_rate and calcium_this_pairing.
        Calculate the sum of synaptic_weight and the result.
        Set synaptic_weight to the result.
    End the conditional block.
End the loop.
Display synaptic_weight to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because the result is one comparison between two conditions and the gating logic reads almost as English.

RGS14_CLAMP, AVP_GAIN = 0.25, 4.0    # calcium surviving the clamp, and its relief
LTP_THRESHOLD, RATE = 3.0, 0.1       # gate level and learning rate
def run(pairings=10, vasopressin=False):
    clamp = min(RGS14_CLAMP * (AVP_GAIN if vasopressin else 1.0), 1.0)
    weight, calcium_total = 1.0, 0.0
    for _ in range(pairings):
        calcium = clamp                      # calcium entering one CA2 spine
        calcium_total += calcium
        if calcium_total > LTP_THRESHOLD:    # plasticity only above the gate
            weight += RATE * calcium
    return weight, calcium_total
for avp in (False, True):
    w, ca = run(vasopressin=avp)
    print("%s: calcium %.2f, weight %.2f"
          % ("vasopressin present" if avp else "vasopressin absent ", ca, w))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A large pyramid with no thorny excrescences, wearing a perineuronal net like chain mail, fired by its distal tuft, deaf to ordinary potentiation, and listening for vasopressin.

CHAPTER 42. CA1 PYRAMIDAL CELLS (Expectation-Checking Place Neuron) (COG)

Fourteen million of these cells per side are the hippocampus's voice to the rest of the brain, and a single spike in one of their dendrites can build a place field in a single pass.

CA1 (Cornu Ammonis 1) is a third address in the same historical Latin series, saying nothing about the cell's underlying work.

Expectation-Checking names the cell's position in the circuit: it sits where the prediction assembled upstream arrives on proximal dendrites while raw sensory data arrives from entorhinal layer 3 on distal dendrites - an excess of sensory input over prediction triggers a dendritic calcium plateau that acts as a local error signal driving plasticity.

Place names its best-documented output: the classic place field discovered in these cells in 1971.

Neuron marks it as the primary excitatory projection cell of the field.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The CA1 pyramidal cell is the main output of the hippocampus, comparing what CA3 predicts, on its middle dendrites, with what the cortex reports, on its tuft, and many are place cells firing in one spot only.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

One thick apical trunk rises to a spreading tuft, throwing off oblique branches, while basal dendrites descend, and a rat cell carries about 30,000 excitatory and 1,700 inhibitory synapses.

SIZE:

The cell body is 15 to 25 micrometres across, the apical dendrite spans 600 micrometres, and dendritic length totals some 12 millimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Deep and superficial cells differ in gene expression, inhibition and place field stability, and a second axis runs from CA1 near CA2 to CA1 near the subiculum.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies fill stratum pyramidale of CA1, dendrites occupy stratum oriens, radiatum and lacunosum-moleculare, and axons collect in the alveus.

The interfaces are the CA2 border, where the marker proteins end, and the subicular border, where the layer loosens.

NUMBER:

About 14 million per hemisphere and 28 million in total, roughly 0.033 percent of the nervous system, or one neuron in 3,000.

Human stereology reports 12 to 16 million per hippocampus, so confidence is good.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage is near minus 68 millivolts, input resistance 50 to 100 megaohms, and channels that grow denser with distance from the cell body give a resonance at theta rhythm, 4 to 10 cycles per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Schaffer collaterals from CA3 land in stratum radiatum, entorhinal layer three fibres in the tuft, the nucleus reuniens and amygdala add more, and a dozen interneuron types control the cell.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The axon runs in the alveus and divides into a local branch and long branches to subiculum, entorhinal cortex, prefrontal cortex, amygdala, accumbens and septum.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the transmitter, and acetylcholine, noradrenaline, serotonin and dopamine decide whether the cell is encoding or recalling.

MYELINATION:

Long-range axons in the alveus and fimbria are myelinated, and even the local axon carries patchy myelin, sharpening ripple timing.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Dense NMDA receptors and a large calcium load with every burst make this the first cell to die after cardiac arrest, long known to pathologists as the Sommer sector.

PLASTICITY:

Long-term potentiation and depression were discovered here, but the deeper mechanism is behavioural timescale plasticity: one dendritic plateau strengthens every synapse active within seconds of it.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytes supply D-serine, the partner molecule the NMDA receptor needs, so plasticity is gated by glia, and lactate, without which long-term memory fails.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove CA1 and the hippocampus becomes a sealed room: prediction is never checked against reality and no new episodic memory is laid down.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Dendritic plateaus turn a millisecond event into a seconds-long eligibility trace, action potentials travel backward into the dendrites to report that the cell fired, and messenger ribonucleic acid is translated locally so one branch can change alone.

These cells also control their own blood supply and tell precursor cells to adjust myelin.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: 30,000 excitatory synapses, mostly Schaffer collaterals, some 3,000 in the tuft from entorhinal layer three, plus 1,700 inhibitory synapses.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate onto subiculum, entorhinal cortex, prefrontal cortex, amygdala, accumbens, septum.

• STATE: membrane voltage, per-branch dendritic voltage, plateau flag, per-synapse eligibility trace and weight.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 68 millivolts, threshold minus 50 millivolts, resistance 60 megaohms, plateau 100 to 500 milliseconds.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 to 30 milliseconds, plateau 100 to 500 milliseconds, eligibility trace 1 to 3 seconds, ripple period 5 to 7 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE: first to die in cardiac arrest and stroke, lost early in Alzheimer's disease, where ripples become disordered and consolidation fails.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to build a place field in one lap by letting a single dendritic plateau strengthen every input active in the seconds before it.
Create a number variable called plateau_step with initial value 120.
Create a number variable called learning_rate with initial value 0.6.
Create a number variable called current_step with initial value 0.
Create a list variable called weights with initial value empty list.
Create a list variable called eligibility with initial value empty list.
While current_step is less than 200, repeat the following steps.
    For each trace in eligibility, perform the following operations.
        Calculate the product of trace and 0.967.
        Set trace to the result.
    End the loop.
    Calculate the sum of the eligibility at the active input and 0.05.
    Set the eligibility at the active input to the result.
    Check if current_step is equal to plateau_step.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        For each position in weights, perform the following operations.
            Calculate the product of learning_rate and the eligibility at position.
            Calculate the sum of the weight at position and the result.
            Set the weight at position to the result.
        End the loop.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the sum of current_step and 1.
    Set current_step to the result.
End the loop.
Display the position of the largest weight to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because one-shot place field formation is best shown as a weight profile.

import math
DT, LAP, N, TAU, RATE = 0.05, 10.0, 100, 1.5, 0.6   # seconds, bins, trace, rate
PLATEAU_STEP = 120                       # the plateau falls at 6.0 s, that is bin 60
w, elig, plat = [0.0] * N, [0.0] * N, 0.0
for step in range(int(LAP / DT)):
    idx = min(int(N * step * DT / LAP), N - 1)      # input active now
    decay = math.exp(-DT / TAU)
    elig = [e * decay for e in elig]                # seconds-long eligibility
    plat *= decay
    elig[idx] += DT
    if step == PLATEAU_STEP:                        # one plateau, one lap
        w = [w[i] + RATE * elig[i] for i in range(N)]
        plat = 1.0
    w[idx] += RATE * plat * DT                      # inputs after it count too
field = [i for i in range(N) if w[i] >= max(w) / 2.0]
print("peak at bin %d, plateau at bin 60" % max(range(N), key=lambda i: w[i]))
print("field spans bins %d to %d" % (min(field), max(field)))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A tall pyramid in the widest cell layer of the hippocampus, thirty thousand excitatory synapses on one tree, a tuft listening to cortex while the middle listens to CA3, and one plateau that invents a place field in a single pass.

CHAPTER 43. SUBICULAR PYRAMIDAL CELLS (Memory-Sending Burst Neuron) (COG)

Everything the hippocampus ever learns must leave the building through one narrow gate, and this cell is the guard standing in it.

Subicular comes from the Latin for "lying underneath" - a purely positional term that describes location rather than function.

Memory-Sending names the primary job: this cell serves as the principal output stage of the hippocampal formation, broadcasting finished spatial and memory representations out to the entorhinal cortex, presubiculum, retrosplenial cortex, nucleus accumbens, amygdala, and hypothalamus.

Burst names its distinctive physiological voice: these cells fire low-threshold packets of spikes riding on a calcium-driven afterdepolarization - packets that carry significantly more spatial information than single isolated spikes.

Neuron marks it as a principal excitatory projection cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The subiculum is the strip of tissue where the hippocampus ends, and its pyramidal cells (nerve cells with triangular bodies) are the principal output neurons of the whole hippocampal formation.

They take the finished product of hippocampal computation from area CA1 and broadcast it to more than a dozen distant structures, often as a burst, a tight packet of two to five spikes that a target cell cannot ignore.

Many are boundary vector cells, firing whenever a wall lies at a preferred distance and compass direction from the body.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

One thick apical dendrite (a receiving branch) rises into the molecular layer and fans into a tuft, while shorter basal dendrites spread below, all of them studded with spines, the knobs that carry excitatory synapses.

Unlike the razor-thin cell band of CA1, the subicular pyramidal layer is thick and loose, so cell bodies scatter through several hundred micrometres of depth.

SIZE:

The cell body is roughly 15 to 25 micrometres across, about a quarter the width of a human hair.

The dendritic tree spans 500 to 800 micrometres and carries an estimated ten thousand or more spines, a figure extrapolated from rodent reconstructions.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Slice recordings give three electrical classes: regular spiking, weak bursting and strong bursting.

In freely moving rats, 102 subicular principal neurons sorted into about 80 percent sparsely bursting and about 20 percent dominantly bursting cells, and the sparse bursters carried the stronger spatial signal.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The subiculum proper, in its molecular, pyramidal and polymorphic layers, sitting at the interface between area CA1 and the presubiculum, a genuine seam where the packed hippocampal band suddenly loosens.

Cells occupy the whole long axis, from the spatial dorsal or posterior subiculum to the emotional and hormonal ventral or anterior subiculum, and lie along a proximal to distal axis that predicts which distant target each cell writes to.

NUMBER:

Unbiased stereology (counting cells in tissue without statistical bias) gives 4.5 million neurons per hemisphere in the human subiculum, with a coefficient of variation of 0.19 across five brains.

Pyramidal cells are perhaps 85 to 90 percent of those, so about 8 million across both hemispheres, roughly 5 percent of the hippocampal formation's 150 million neurons and about 0.01 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons of the nervous system; confidence is good for the total and only fair for the pyramidal fraction.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Rest sits near minus 65 millivolts and threshold near minus 50, with input resistance of 30 to 60 megaohms in rat and a membrane time constant of 15 to 25 milliseconds.

The burst is built by low-threshold Cav3 calcium channels, which only work once the cell has been held quiet long enough for them to recover, and it is terminated by calcium-activated potassium channels.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

The dominant input is a topographically ordered projection from CA1, joined by entorhinal layer III fibres on the apical tuft and by the thalamic nucleus reuniens, amygdala and medial septum.

Outputs reach entorhinal deep layers, presubiculum, parasubiculum, retrosplenial cortex, anterior thalamus, mammillary bodies, nucleus accumbens, prefrontal cortex, amygdala and hypothalamus, while connections between subicular pyramids themselves are sparse.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The axon joins the alveus and enters either the fornix, the great white cable arching under the corpus callosum, or the entorhinal cortex, running centimetres in a human brain.

Single cells tend to specialise on one main distant target rather than spraying all of them.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate, the standard excitatory messenger, acting on AMPA receptors for speed and NMDA receptors for learning.

The cell receives gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the standard inhibitory messenger, plus acetylcholine, noradrenaline, serotonin and, ventrally, dopamine.

MYELINATION:

Yes, and it matters, because these are the only hippocampal formation neurons whose axons routinely travel centimetres.

Fornix axons are thin and thinly myelinated (wrapped in fatty insulation), conducting at roughly 1 to 5 metres per second.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

High, and dominated by the long axon rather than the dendrites, since holding ionic gradients along centimetres of thin axon consumes much of the cell's adenosine triphosphate (ATP), the molecule cells spend as energy.

The subiculum tolerates oxygen shortage better than CA1 but is disproportionately lost in ageing, with one influential study reporting a 52 percent decline between ages 13 and 85.

PLASTICITY:

Synapses from CA1 strengthen lastingly, but by different mechanisms in neighbouring cells: bursting cells express a presynaptic increase in transmitter release while regular-firing cells express a classical postsynaptic NMDA-dependent change.

The same input therefore leaves two different kinds of memory trace side by side.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytes here face an unusual chloride problem, because these neurons accumulate chloride readily and glial and neuronal transporters together decide whether GABA calms or excites them.

In human temporal lobe epilepsy the subiculum shows heavy reactive gliosis while its neurons survive, the opposite of the pattern in CA1, and oligodendrocytes matter far more here than in CA1 because of the long axons.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete this cell type and the hippocampus becomes a beautifully organised machine with no output socket.

Its steady, boundary-anchored, environment-invariant flavour of spatial coding, broader and more reliable than the fickle place fields of CA1, would vanish from the brain's sense of where it is.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Burst versus single spike is a code in itself, read as two different symbols by downstream synapses.

Back-propagating action potentials run up the apical dendrite and trigger local calcium spikes, which is how the cell detects that hippocampal and cortical inputs arrived together.

Chloride handling is an active computation: when the transporter KCC2 is lost, as it is in epileptic human tissue, GABA stops inhibiting and starts depolarising, and slices of human subiculum then generate spontaneous rhythmic discharges identical to the interictal spikes on a patient's electroencephalogram.

Ventral cells also drive the hypothalamic stress axis, so this neuron reaches out of the skull and into the bloodstream.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ CA1 excitation, 2000 to 5000 synapses per cell, 0 to 40 hertz per source.

◦ Entorhinal layer III excitation onto the tuft, arriving 3 to 8 milliseconds later.

◦ Local GABA inhibition, 0 to 5 nanosiemens, with a reversal potential set by internal chloride.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Spike trains tagged as single spike or burst, sent to entorhinal cortex, presubiculum, retrosplenial cortex, anterior thalamus, mammillary bodies, accumbens, prefrontal cortex, amygdala and hypothalamus.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts, Cav3 availability from 0 to 1, and a slow potassium brake in millivolts.

◦ Internal chloride concentration in millimolar.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rest minus 65, threshold minus 50, reset minus 58 millivolts.

◦ Input resistance 30 to 60 megaohms, membrane time constant 15 to 25 milliseconds, dominant bursters 20 percent of the population.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 20 milliseconds, intra-burst interval 5 to 15 milliseconds, Cav3 recovery 100 to 200 milliseconds, slow brake 200 to 300 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Loss of KCC2 makes the region the interictal spike generator of temporal lobe epilepsy.

◦ Age-related attrition narrows the only output channel the hippocampus has.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to decide whether a subicular pyramidal cell answers a steady input with single spikes or with a burst.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value minus sixty five.
Create a number variable called calcium boost with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called slow brake with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called calcium available with initial value one.
Create a number variable called clock with initial value zero.
Create a list variable called spike times with initial value empty list.
While clock is less than six hundred, repeat the following steps.
    Set target to the sum of minus forty seven and the difference of calcium boost and slow brake.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the quotient of the difference of target and voltage divided by two hundred.
    Set calcium boost to the product of calcium boost and ninety nine hundredths.
    Check if voltage is greater than or equal to minus fifty.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Add clock to the end of spike times.
        Set voltage to minus fifty eight.
        Set calcium boost to the sum of calcium boost and the product of fourteen and calcium available.
        Set calcium available to the product of calcium available and forty five hundredths.
        Set slow brake to the sum of slow brake and three.
    End the conditional block.
    Set clock to the sum of clock and one tenth.
End the loop.
Get the total number of items in spike times.
Display the total number of items in spike times to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python is chosen because the whole burst mechanism fits in one readable loop
# with no libraries, so a beginner can follow the biology line by line.
DT, V_REST, V_TH, V_RESET, TAU_M, R_IN = 0.1, -65.0, -50.0, -58.0, 20.0, 45.0
B_STEP, TAU_B, A_STEP, TAU_A, TAU_REC = 14.0, 40.0, 3.0, 250.0, 150.0
def simulate(current_na, ms=1200.0, has_cav3=True):
    v, b, a, avail, spikes = V_REST, 0.0, 0.0, 1.0, []
    for i in range(int(ms / DT)):
        t = i * DT
        v += ((V_REST - v) + R_IN * current_na + b - a) / TAU_M * DT
        b -= b / TAU_B * DT            # low threshold calcium depolarisation
        a -= a / TAU_A * DT            # calcium activated potassium brake
        if v < -60.0:
            avail += (1.0 - avail) / TAU_REC * DT    # only a quiet cell rearms
        if v >= V_TH:
            spikes.append(round(t, 1))
            v, a = V_RESET, a + A_STEP
            if has_cav3:
                b += B_STEP * avail    # the boost fades as channels inactivate
                avail *= 0.45
    return spikes
def gaps(s):
    return [round(s[i + 1] - s[i], 1) for i in range(len(s) - 1)]
burster = simulate(0.45)
print("burster:", len(burster), "spikes, first gaps in ms", gaps(burster)[:6])
print("regular cell:", len(simulate(0.75, has_cav3=False)), "spikes")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Inject a steady current and a large fraction of these cells answer not with an even train but with a tight cluster of spikes riding on a hump of depolarisation that only appears if the cell was first held quiet.

Add the anatomy, a spiny pyramidal cell in a thick loose layer just past the end of CA1 whose axon leaves the hippocampus altogether, and the identification is certain.

CHAPTER 44. ENTORHINAL STELLATE CELLS (Distance-And-Direction Mapping Neuron) (COG)

Somewhere behind your eyes a few hundred thousand cells lay an invisible sheet of triangular graph paper over the room you are standing in, and this is the cell that draws it.

Entorhinal Stellate combines a brain address with the Latin word for star-shaped - describing a silhouette at a location without explaining its computation.

Distance-And-Direction names what these cells actually measure: as the core population of grid cells, they fire at the vertices of a repeating triangular lattice dictated by an animal's self-motion and heading rather than visual scenery.

Mapping names the faculty: providing a universal metric of space laid over any environment an animal enters.

Neuron marks it as a principal excitatory grid cell whose shared local inhibitory network stabilizes the spatial lattice across the entire layer.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The stellate cell is the star-shaped principal neuron of layer II of the medial entorhinal cortex (MEC), the patch of cortex that feeds the hippocampus, and star-shaped means its dendrites radiate in all directions instead of forming one trunk.

Many are grid cells, firing whenever the animal crosses any vertex of a perfect triangular lattice covering the floor, so the cell is a ruler rather than a landmark detector.

Stellate cells are the main source of the perforant path to the dentate gyrus and CA3, the cable that carries the outside world into memory.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A polygonal cell body throws out many thick primary dendrites of similar calibre in a starburst, several of them reaching layer I to collect long-range cortical input, all of them spiny.

They occupy the spaces between round clusters of calbindin-positive pyramidal cells, producing the pattern anatomists call islands and ocean, with pyramidal islands surrounded by a stellate ocean.

SIZE:

The cell body is roughly 15 to 25 micrometres across.

The dendritic tree spans about 400 to 600 micrometres horizontally and reaches the pial surface vertically, a total dendritic length of several millimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

The meaningful subtyping is by position along the dorsal to ventral axis, because position sets the scale of the grid: dorsal cells draw fine grids with vertices 30 to 40 centimetres apart and ventral cells draw grids metres across.
