The scales are not continuous but fall into four or five discrete modules, and the ratio between one module's scale and the next is about 1.4, close to the square root of two.

Roughly 53 percent of layer II principal cells are reelin-positive stellate cells, about 34 percent are calbindin-positive pyramidal cells, and about 13 percent of layer II neurons are interneurons.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Layer II of the medial entorhinal cortex, corresponding in humans to the posteromedial entorhinal cortex on the medial surface of the temporal lobe.

Three interfaces matter: with layer I above, where long-range inputs arrive; with the lateral entorhinal cortex in front, where the map of space gives way to the catalogue of things; and with the presubiculum and parasubiculum behind, which deliver the head direction signal.

NUMBER:

Human stereology gives about 650,000 layer II neurons in the whole entorhinal cortex per hemisphere, and about 7 million in the entorhinal cortex overall.

If the posteromedial division holds roughly 40 percent of layer II and reelin-positive stellate cells are about half of its principal cells, the estimate is 100,000 to 300,000 per hemisphere, so 0.2 to 0.6 million across both hemispheres, about 0.3 percent of the hippocampal formation and about 0.0005 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons of the nervous system.

Confidence is low, because the human medial and lateral divisions are still disputed and the reelin fraction has never been counted properly in humans.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Inject a negative current and the voltage dips and then creeps back up while the current is still flowing, a rebound called sag, produced by hyperpolarization-activated cyclic nucleotide-gated (HCN) channels, mainly HCN1, carrying a current called Ih.

Because Ih opposes slow voltage changes but not fast ones, the membrane becomes a band-pass filter that responds most strongly to inputs oscillating at 4 to 10 hertz, a property called resonance, and the cells also hum with subthreshold oscillations at that frequency.

Blocking Ih abolishes the sag, and deleting HCN1 abolishes resonance below 4 hertz and flattens the dorsal to ventral gradient of resonance frequency that runs in parallel with the gradient of grid spacing.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs arrive from presubiculum and parasubiculum carrying head direction, from postrhinal and retrosplenial cortex carrying visual scenes, from the medial septum carrying theta and acetylcholine, and from the lateral entorhinal cortex carrying objects.

The output is the perforant path, terminating in the outer two thirds of the dentate molecular layer and in stratum lacunosum-moleculare of CA3.

Direct excitatory connections between stellate cells are very rare, so they are wired to each other almost entirely through inhibitory interneurons, and that recurrent inhibition is now the leading candidate mechanism for generating the grid itself.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The main axon descends through the deep layers, turns through the angular bundle and perforates the subiculum, which is why the pathway is called perforant, travelling millimetres to centimetres to reach the dentate gyrus.

Local collaterals stay in layer II and target interneurons.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate at the output, acting on AMPA and NMDA receptors on granule cells.

Acetylcholine from the medial septum acts on muscarinic receptors to shift resonance frequency and can switch the cell into persistent firing, and the cell also receives GABA locally and is modulated by noradrenaline, serotonin and dopamine.

MYELINATION:

Yes, thinly, along the perforant path axon.

Conduction takes a few milliseconds, and the precision of that delay matters because the dentate gyrus must judge whether cortical input and hippocampal feedback fell in the same theta cycle.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

High and unusual, because HCN channels are open at rest, so a standing sodium and potassium leak must be undone continuously by the sodium potassium pump.

A stellate cell therefore burns energy simply to sit still, which is one plausible reason these neurons are the first in the cortex to die in Alzheimer's disease.

PLASTICITY:

Perforant path synapses onto granule cells are the classic site of long-term potentiation, first demonstrated in the rabbit in 1973, and they remain the textbook memory synapse.

The cell's own resonance frequency is plastic minute by minute through cholinergic modulation, and grid patterns rescale when the walls of a familiar room are moved, so the ruler is adjustable.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytes in this layer wrap the most tau-vulnerable neurons in the brain, and layer-specific astrogliosis here is severe, with swelling, altered protein expression and reduced glutamate clearance.

Microglia in this exact patch are implicated in passing tau protein from neuron to neuron along the perforant path, making the local immune cells an active vehicle for the disease rather than bystanders.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove stellate cells and the entorhinal cortex loses its metric.

It would still report what is present and which way the head points, but no longer how far you have walked or in what direction, so the hippocampus would receive a picture with no scale bar, and the theta hum of the region would fade with it.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The membrane itself computes, because resonance means identical input delivered at 8 hertz produces a far larger voltage swing than the same input at 1 hertz.

Persistent firing, driven by a calcium-activated non-specific cation current under cholinergic control, lets a single cell hold a memory for tens of seconds with no synaptic help at all.

Dendrites in layer I integrate locally, far from the cell body, and back-propagating spikes tag them, while gap junctions between the interneurons that link stellate cells add a layer of electrical coupling, and acetylcholine reaches the whole layer as a diffuse cloud rather than at precise synapses.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Running speed as a scalar 0 to 1 and head direction as an angle 0 to 360 degrees.

◦ Cortical excitation in layer I, 1000 to 5000 synapses per cell.

◦ Inhibition from local fast-spiking interneurons, 0 to 10 nanosiemens, the dominant recurrent term.

◦ Acetylcholine level 0 to 1, setting resonance frequency and persistent firing.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Spikes to dentate granule cells and CA3 along the perforant path.

◦ Local excitation of interneurons, which is how stellate cells reach one another.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts and HCN gating variable 0 to 1.

◦ Theta phase 0 to 2 pi and grid phase as a two-dimensional position inside the lattice unit cell.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rest about minus 62 millivolts, input resistance 30 to 60 megaohms, membrane time constant 8 to 15 milliseconds.

◦ Resonance 7 to 10 hertz dorsally and 3 to 5 hertz ventrally; grid spacing 30 centimetres to 3 metres in four or five modules separated by a factor of about 1.4.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane about 10 milliseconds, HCN activation 20 to 200 milliseconds by position, theta cycle 125 to 250 milliseconds, persistent firing tens of seconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Layer II of the entorhinal cortex is the first cortical region to accumulate neurofibrillary tangles, and in very mild Alzheimer's disease it has already lost about 60 percent of its neurons, rising to about 90 percent in severe disease.

◦ The clinical result is getting lost in familiar places, often the first symptom a family notices.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to find the input frequency at which a medial entorhinal stellate cell rings loudest, which is its resonance frequency.
Create a number variable called best frequency with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called best swing with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called test frequency with initial value one.
While test frequency is less than or equal to twenty, repeat the following steps.
    Create a number variable called voltage with initial value zero.
    Create a number variable called h gate with initial value zero.
    Create a number variable called highest with initial value zero.
    Create a number variable called lowest with initial value zero.
    Create a number variable called clock with initial value zero.
    While clock is less than ten thousand, repeat the following steps.
        Set stimulus to the product of five hundredths and the sine of the product of clock and test frequency.
        Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the quotient of the difference of stimulus and the sum of voltage and h gate divided by one hundred and twenty.
        Set h gate to the sum of h gate and the quotient of the difference of voltage and h gate divided by seven hundred and fifty.
        Check if both clock is greater than six thousand and voltage is greater than highest are true.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set highest to voltage.
        End the conditional block.
        Check if both clock is greater than six thousand and voltage is less than lowest are true.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set lowest to voltage.
        End the conditional block.
        Set clock to the sum of clock and one.
    End the loop.
    Check if the difference of highest and lowest is greater than best swing.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set best swing to the difference of highest and lowest.
        Set best frequency to test frequency.
    End the conditional block.
    Set test frequency to the sum of test frequency and one half.
End the loop.
Display best frequency to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

import math
# Python is chosen because the resonance test is a short numerical loop whose only
# mathematics is a sine wave, so nothing important is hidden inside a library.
DT, TAU_M, R_M, G_H, AMP = 0.0001, 0.012, 50.0, 1.5, 0.05
def swing(freq_hz, tau_h, cycles=10):
    v, h = 0.0, 0.0           # voltage in millivolts, HCN gate in the same units
    duration = cycles / freq_hz
    hi, lo = -1e9, 1e9
    for i in range(int(duration / DT)):
        t = i * DT
        current = AMP * math.sin(2.0 * math.pi * freq_hz * t)
        v += ((-v - G_H * h + R_M * current) / TAU_M) * DT
        h += ((v - h) / tau_h) * DT
        if t > 0.6 * duration:                # ignore the settling transient
            hi, lo = max(hi, v), min(lo, v)
    return (hi - lo) / 2.0
for label, tau_h in (("dorsal cell", 0.075), ("ventral cell", 0.200)):
    best_f, best_a, f = 0.0, 0.0, 1.0
    while f <= 20.0:
        a = swing(f, tau_h)
        if a > best_a:
            best_a, best_f = a, f
        f += 0.5
    print(label, "rings loudest at", round(best_f, 1), "hertz")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Hyperpolarise it and watch the voltage sag back up while the current is still flowing, then sweep an oscillating current through it and find a clear peak in the theta band.

Confirm it anatomically by the starburst of equal dendrites and its position in the ocean between the calbindin islands of layer II, and molecularly by reelin.

CHAPTER 45. ENTORHINAL FAN CELLS (What-Where-When Binding Neuron) (COG)

If the stellate cell draws the map, the fan cell writes what happened on it.

Entorhinal Fan combines a cortical address with a shape word describing the broad spray of dendrites into Layer 1 - a vivid visual image that tells a student nothing about the cell's informational content.

What-Where-When names the three distinct strands of experience this cell joins together: silencing these cells selectively abolishes memory for object-place-context associations while leaving plain object recognition intact.

Binding names the operation: gluing these separate sensory and contextual streams into a single unified episodic memory.

Neuron marks it as a principal excitatory cell feeding the dentate gyrus and downstream recall machinery.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The fan cell is the principal excitatory neuron of the superficial half of layer 2 of the lateral entorhinal cortex (LEC), the region that supplies the hippocampus with objects, odours, actions and elapsed time rather than with distance and direction.

Its job is to bind an object to the place and context where it was met and hand that bundle to the dentate gyrus, and silencing fan cells in mice leaves them able to notice a new context but unable to recognise a new combination of object, place and context.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A polygonal cell body sends a spray of spiny primary dendrites upward into layer I in the shape of a hand fan, with few or no basal dendrites, which is a real difference from the stellate cell next door.

SIZE:

The cell body is roughly 15 to 20 micrometres across and the dendritic fan spans 300 to 600 micrometres while reaching the pial surface, giving a few millimetres of dendrite in total.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Layer 2 divides into a superficial sublayer 2a holding reelin-positive fan cells, which project to the dentate gyrus, and a deeper sublayer 2b holding calbindin-positive pyramidal cells, which do not.

Within the fan cell class there is a gradient of dendritic spread and input rather than sharp subtypes, plus minor multipolar and oblique variants.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Layer 2a of the lateral entorhinal cortex, which in humans is the anterolateral entorhinal cortex on the medial temporal lobe just behind the amygdala.

The interfaces that matter are with perirhinal cortex laterally, which supplies object identity, with medial entorhinal cortex medially, which supplies the metric, and with layer I above, where fan cell axons also cross directly into the medial entorhinal cortex and stitch the two maps together.

NUMBER:

From about 650,000 layer II entorhinal neurons per hemisphere, an anterolateral share of roughly 60 percent and a reelin-positive fraction of about half gives 150,000 to 350,000 fan cells per hemisphere, or 0.3 to 0.7 million across both hemispheres.

That is about 0.4 percent of the hippocampal formation and about 0.0006 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons of the nervous system, and confidence is low because the human divisions are disputed and the reelin fraction is uncounted in humans.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Measured in rat, fan cells rest at minus 68.5 plus or minus 1.4 millivolts, with an input resistance of 130.5 plus or minus 11.9 megaohms, a membrane time constant of 24.2 plus or minus 1.7 milliseconds and a sag ratio near 0.85.

That combination of high resistance and slow time constant makes the fan cell excitable and patient, an integrator rather than a resonator, and it fires regular accommodating trains that under acetylcholine can become persistent firing lasting more than thirty seconds.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs come from perirhinal and piriform cortex carrying objects and odours, from the amygdala carrying emotional value, and from the medial septum carrying acetylcholine and theta.

The output is the lateral perforant path, ending in the outermost third of the dentate molecular layer and in CA3 just superficial to the medial path, so a granule cell dendrite receives what and where in two adjacent stripes.

Direct excitation between fan cells is rare while inhibitory connections through local interneurons are common, so the network computes by competition and produces a sparse code.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The main axon runs through the angular bundle, perforates the subiculum and travels millimetres to centimetres to the dentate gyrus, with a second branch turning medially into layer I of the medial entorhinal cortex and local collaterals contacting interneurons.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate at the output, with acetylcholine as the decisive modulator through muscarinic receptors, which is why anticholinergic drugs blur episodic memory.

The cells also secrete reelin itself into the extracellular space as a signalling protein, and they receive GABA locally along with noradrenaline and serotonin.

MYELINATION:

Yes, thinly, along the lateral perforant path, whose conduction delay is slightly longer than that of the medial path, a timing offset that may help the dentate gyrus separate what from where inside a single theta cycle.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Moderate to high, since the high input resistance saves energy at rest but the long axon and heavy perirhinal traffic keep demand up, and the anterolateral entorhinal cortex shows the earliest measurable fall in glucose use in people who later develop Alzheimer's disease.

PLASTICITY:

The lateral perforant path shows long-term potentiation with an unusual opioid-sensitive component, because enkephalin-containing fibres run alongside, and the cell's own persistent firing is a short-term memory held in the membrane rather than in synapses.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The glia here are not structurally exotic, and saying so plainly matters.

What is special is timing, because the transentorhinal and lateral entorhinal region is where abnormal tau protein first appears in the human brain, and astrocytes and microglia in this exact patch are the first cortical glia to turn inflammatory and to help carry tau from neuron to neuron.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove fan cells and the entorhinal cortex still knows where the body is but no longer what is in the room, so the hippocampus receives a perfectly scaled and entirely empty map.

You would keep the ability to walk to the table and lose the ability to remember putting your keys on it.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Persistent firing, driven by a calcium-activated non-specific cation current switched on by acetylcholine, lets one cell hold an input in a self-sustaining loop with no synaptic support, a genuine single-cell memory.

The fan-shaped dendrites integrate locally in layer I on inputs that never meet at the cell body, reelin is released into the extracellular space to remodel nearby synapses without a synapse of its own, and the dominance of inhibition over excitation makes the population compute by competition.

Local blood flow tracks fan cell activity closely, which is what human imaging of the anterolateral entorhinal cortex is actually measuring.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Object and odour feature vectors from perirhinal and piriform cortex, 1000 to 4000 synapses per cell.

◦ Amygdala value signal 0 to 1, and acetylcholine level 0 to 1 which gates persistent firing.

◦ Inhibition from local interneurons, 0 to 8 nanosiemens, the dominant recurrent term.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Sparse spike trains to dentate granule cells and CA3 along the lateral perforant path, plus a direct projection into layer I of the medial entorhinal cortex.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts, adaptation in millivolts, shared inhibitory pool in millivolts.

◦ Persistent firing calcium store 0 to 1.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rest minus 68.5 millivolts, input resistance about 130 megaohms, membrane time constant about 24 milliseconds, sag ratio about 0.85.

◦ Target sparseness 5 to 15 percent of the local population active at once.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 24 milliseconds, inhibitory pool 5 to 10 milliseconds, adaptation 100 to 300 milliseconds, persistent firing 30 seconds or more.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Tau pathology starts here, so fan cells are among the first human neurons to tangle and die, and the earliest clinical sign is failing to recall what happened where.

◦ Loss of cholinergic input removes persistent firing and with it the ability to hold an object in mind across a delay.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn a broad pattern of cortical input into the sparse fan cell code that the dentate gyrus expects, using shared inhibition to enforce competition.
Create a list variable called voltages with initial value empty list.
Create a list variable called drives with initial value empty list.
Create a number variable called inhibition with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called winners with initial value zero.
Repeat the following steps exactly forty times.
    Add minus sixty eight point five to the end of voltages.
    Add a randomly chosen current between one tenth and twenty two hundredths to the end of drives.
End the loop.
Repeat the following steps exactly two hundred times.
    Create a number variable called fired this step with initial value zero.
    For each cell in voltages, perform the following operations.
        Set target to the difference of the sum of minus sixty eight point five and the product of one hundred and thirty and the drive for that cell and inhibition.
        Set cell to the sum of cell and the quotient of the difference of target and cell divided by twenty four.
        Check if cell is greater than or equal to minus forty five.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set cell to minus sixty.
            Set fired this step to the sum of fired this step and one.
            Set winners to the sum of winners and one.
        End the conditional block.
    End the loop.
    Set inhibition to the product of the sum of inhibition and the product of two point two and fired this step and eighty seven hundredths.
End the loop.
Display winners to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

import random
# Python is chosen because competition between cells is clearest as a plain loop
# over a list, with no matrix algebra hiding the winner take few behaviour.
random.seed(7)
N, DT, TAU_M, R_IN = 40, 1.0, 24.0, 130.0
V_REST, V_TH, V_RESET, TAU_I, W_I = -68.5, -45.0, -60.0, 8.0, 2.2
def run(drive, ms=200.0):
    v, counts, inhib = [V_REST] * N, [0] * N, 0.0
    for _ in range(int(ms / DT)):
        fired = 0
        for j in range(N):
            target = V_REST + R_IN * drive[j] - inhib   # megaohms times nanoamps
            v[j] += (target - v[j]) / TAU_M * DT
            if v[j] >= V_TH:
                v[j], fired = V_RESET, fired + 1
                counts[j] += 1
        inhib += W_I * fired          # one shared pool of feedback interneurons
        inhib -= inhib / TAU_I * DT
    return counts
counts = run([random.uniform(0.10, 0.22) for _ in range(N)])
active = sum(1 for c in counts if c > 0)
print("active cells:", active, "of", N, "sparseness", round(active / N, 3))
print("busiest cell fired", max(counts), "times")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A polygonal cell in the top half of layer 2 of the lateral entorhinal cortex whose dendrites open upward like a hand fan, with few or no basal dendrites, staining for reelin and projecting to the dentate gyrus.

Electrically the giveaway is a high input resistance near 130 megaohms combined with only a modest sag, which separates it cleanly from the low-resistance, strongly sagging stellate cell a few millimetres away.

CHAPTER 46. PARVALBUMIN BASKET CELLS (Clockwork-Precise Soma Brake Neuron) (COG)

This is the metronome of the hippocampus: a cell that fires two hundred times a second without tiring and wraps its neighbours' cell bodies in woven baskets of axon terminals.

Parvalbumin Basket combines a chemical protein marker with a descriptive shape word, describing a molecular barcode and a physical terminal shape without explaining network function.

Clockwork-Precise names the cell's defining signature: these fast-spiking interneurons fire with sub-millisecond timing, acting as the primary clocks of the hippocampal circuit by generating gamma and ripple rhythms.

Soma Brake names the target and effect: a swift, powerful inhibitory chop delivered directly onto the soma and axon initial segment that can veto an outgoing action potential outright.

Neuron marks it as the primary fast-spiking inhibitory interneuron enforcing rhythmic temporal precision.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

A basket cell is an inhibitory interneuron, a nerve cell with a short local axon whose job is to quieten others, and this one contains parvalbumin (PV), a fast calcium-binding protein that lets it recover quickly between spikes.

It targets the cell body and proximal dendrites of pyramidal and granule cells, the place where the decision to fire is settled, and by delivering a sharp pulse of inhibition every 15 to 30 milliseconds it carves continuous excitation into discrete windows, which is the gamma rhythm of roughly 30 to 80 cycles per second.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A round or oval smooth cell body in or just below the principal cell layer, with aspiny dendrites running vertically across every layer so the cell samples all incoming pathways, and an axon that branches explosively into curled baskets around its targets.

Most of these cells are wrapped in a perineuronal net, a lattice of sugar-rich extracellular matrix fitted around the cell body like chain mail.

SIZE:

The cell body is 15 to 25 micrometres across and the dendrites span 500 to 900 micrometres, but the axon is the astonishing part.

A single reconstructed CA1 parvalbumin basket cell has more than 40 millimetres of axon carrying over 10,000 boutons in a volume about a millimetre across, containing roughly 10,000 pyramidal cells, of which it contacts about 10 percent, placing about half a dozen synapses on each of the 1500 to 2500 cells it chooses.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

CA3 cells bias their dendrites towards the mossy fibre input while CA1 cells spread evenly, CA1 cells inhibit deep pyramidal cells more strongly than superficial ones, and a rare minority project long range to the medial septum or across the hippocampal fissure.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Stratum pyramidale and stratum oriens of CA1, CA2 and CA3, the granule cell layer and subgranular zone of the dentate gyrus, the subicular pyramidal layer and layers II and III of the entorhinal cortex.

The cell body sits at the interface between the principal cell layer and the layer beneath it, exactly where the shortest axon can reach the most cell bodies.

NUMBER:

Interneurons are about 10 percent of the hippocampal formation's 150 million neurons, so about 15 million across both hemispheres, of which parvalbumin cells are commonly put at 20 to 25 percent and basket cells at half to two thirds of those.

That gives 1.5 to 2.5 million parvalbumin basket cells across both hemispheres, about 1 to 1.7 percent of the hippocampal formation and about 0.002 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons of the nervous system, with fair confidence because the proportions come from rodent immunohistochemistry.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Fast-spiking is the defining property: action potentials about 0.3 milliseconds wide, a large fast afterhyperpolarisation, and sustained rates above 100 and often above 200 spikes per second with almost no slowing.

The speed comes from Kv3-family potassium channels and Nav1.1 sodium channels, and the low input resistance of 60 to 120 megaohms with a short membrane time constant of 8 to 12 milliseconds makes the cell a coincidence detector that fires only when many inputs arrive within a few milliseconds.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs come from local pyramidal cells, which closes the loop that generates gamma, from CA3 and entorhinal afferents, from the medial septum and from other parvalbumin cells, and the excitatory synapses onto them are unusually fast and calcium-permeable because their AMPA receptors lack the GluA2 subunit.

Outputs land on somata and proximal dendrites, released by P/Q-type calcium channels sitting within tens of nanometres of the release machinery, producing an inhibitory current that decays in 2 to 5 milliseconds through GABA-A receptors containing the alpha1 subunit.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

More than 40 millimetres of thin axon, 0.2 to 0.5 micrometres across, conducting at roughly 0.2 to 0.5 metres per second, which crosses the cell's whole territory in under two milliseconds and so delivers inhibition that is effectively simultaneous across the local population.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's main inhibitory messenger, released synchronously and with high probability, alongside parvalbumin as a calcium buffer rather than a transmitter, with acetylcholine exciting the cell and opioids inhibiting it.

MYELINATION:

Partially, which is a genuine surprise because interneuron axons were long assumed to be bare, and the short myelin patches between branch points are thought to speed and synchronise the delivery of inhibition across the cell's wide territory.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

The highest in the hippocampal formation, because firing at 100 to 200 spikes per second means pumping enormous quantities of sodium and potassium, and these cells carry many more and larger mitochondria than their neighbours, enriched in cytochrome c oxidase and other electron transport chain proteins.

That same machinery generates reactive oxygen species, so the cells are the first to be damaged by oxidative stress.

PLASTICITY:

Synapses onto these cells potentiate and depress by rules that often do not need NMDA receptors, and the perineuronal net around the cell body closes at the end of development, ending the critical period during which the local circuit can be rewired.

Digesting the net with an enzyme reopens plasticity in adult tissue, which makes this cell type a switch controlling how changeable a whole region is.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The perineuronal net is the special support system, built from aggrecan, brevican and related molecules on a hyaluronan backbone by astrocytes and the neuron together, and it stabilises synapses, buffers the storm of potassium and calcium produced by fast firing, and shields the cell from oxidative damage.

Astrocytes here also matter unusually for supplying lactate as fuel during sustained gamma activity, and microglia prune these synapses actively and, in disease, excessively.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Take out parvalbumin basket cells and the hippocampus loses its clock: excitation would still arrive and memories would still be written, but nothing would happen at a particular moment.

Gamma would disappear, spike timing would smear from one millisecond to tens of milliseconds, sharp-wave ripples would collapse, and the region would become epileptic within seconds because nothing else can restrain runaway excitation that fast.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

They are electrically coupled to one another by connexin 36 gap junctions, so they share subthreshold voltage directly and synchronise faster than chemical synapses allow, and their large extracellular currents create ephaptic effects in which one cell's electric field nudges its neighbours with no synapse at all.

They control local blood flow, so the surge in blood oxygen during gamma is largely their doing, they instruct myelination of their own axons by signalling to oligodendrocyte precursors according to activity, and they are metabolically coupled to astrocytes through the lactate shuttle.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Fast excitation from local pyramidal cells and CA3 or entorhinal afferents, 5000 to 15000 synapses per cell, rise time under 0.5 milliseconds.

◦ Inhibition from other parvalbumin and from cholecystokinin cells, 0 to 20 nanosiemens.

◦ Gap junction coupling to neighbours, coupling coefficient 0.02 to 0.10.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Fast GABA-A inhibition onto somata and proximal dendrites of 1500 to 2500 principal cells, about six synapses each, plus inhibition of other interneurons.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts, refractory timer in milliseconds, released conductance in nanosiemens.

◦ Intracellular free calcium, buffered by parvalbumin.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rest minus 65 millivolts, threshold minus 52 millivolts, spike width 0.3 milliseconds, refractory period 1 millisecond.

◦ Input resistance 60 to 120 megaohms, membrane time constant 8 to 12 milliseconds, maximum sustained rate 150 to 250 spikes per second.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 9 milliseconds, output synapse decay 2 to 5 milliseconds, synaptic delay 0.5 to 1 millisecond, gamma cycle 12 to 30 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ In schizophrenia these cells lose parvalbumin and their nets under oxidative stress, gamma synchrony falls and working memory fails.

◦ In Alzheimer's disease reduced Nav1.1 slows them and gamma power falls, and in epilepsy their loss removes the only inhibition fast enough to stop a seizure spreading.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to show how fast spiking parvalbumin basket cells inhibiting one another settle into a gamma rhythm.
Create a list variable called voltages with initial value empty list.
Create a number variable called shared inhibition with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called clock with initial value zero.
Repeat the following steps exactly one hundred times.
    Add a randomly chosen voltage between minus sixty five and minus fifty five to the end of voltages.
End the loop.
While clock is less than five hundred, repeat the following steps.
    Create a number variable called fired this step with initial value zero.
    For each cell in voltages, perform the following operations.
        Set pull to the product of shared inhibition and the difference of minus seventy five and cell.
        Set change to the quotient of the sum of the sum of the difference of minus sixty five and cell and eighteen and pull divided by nine.
        Set cell to the sum of cell and the product of change and five hundredths.
        Check if cell is greater than or equal to minus fifty two.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set cell to minus sixty five.
            Set fired this step to the sum of fired this step and one.
        End the conditional block.
    End the loop.
    Set shared inhibition to the sum of shared inhibition and the quotient of the product of one point five and fired this step divided by one hundred.
    Set shared inhibition to the product of shared inhibition and nine hundred and seventy five thousandths.
    Set clock to the sum of clock and five hundredths.
End the loop.
Display the number of population bursts per second to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

import random
# Python is chosen because a rhythm emerging from mutual inhibition needs no
# numerical library, and the loop makes the feedback obvious to a beginner.
random.seed(3)
N, DT, T, TAU_M = 100, 0.05, 500.0, 9.0
V_REST, V_TH, V_RESET, E_GABA, T_REF = -65.0, -52.0, -65.0, -75.0, 1.0
G_MAX, TAU_SYN, DRIVE = 1.5, 2.0, 18.0
v = [V_REST + random.uniform(0.0, 10.0) for _ in range(N)]
last, g, bins = [-100.0] * N, 0.0, [0] * int(T)
for step in range(int(T / DT)):
    t = step * DT
    g -= g / TAU_SYN * DT
    fired = 0
    for j in range(N):
        if t - last[j] < T_REF:
            continue                        # fast spiking cells need only 1 ms
        v[j] += ((V_REST - v[j]) + DRIVE + g * (E_GABA - v[j])) / TAU_M * DT
        if v[j] >= V_TH:
            v[j], last[j], fired = V_RESET, t, fired + 1
    g += G_MAX * fired / N
    bins[int(t)] += fired
peaks = []
for i in range(1, len(bins) - 1):
    if bins[i] > 0.2 * N and bins[i] >= bins[i - 1] and bins[i] > bins[i + 1]:
        if not peaks or i - peaks[-1] >= 5:
            peaks.append(i)
late = [p for p in peaks if p > T / 2]
if len(late) > 1:
    period = (late[-1] - late[0]) / (len(late) - 1)
    print("population period", round(period, 1), "ms, frequency", round(1000.0 / period), "hertz")
else:
    print("no rhythm emerged with these parameters")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Drive it hard and it fires two hundred knife-thin spikes a second and never tires, it stains for parvalbumin, it sits inside a perineuronal net, and its axon forms baskets around cell bodies rather than climbing dendrites.

No other hippocampal cell combines a smooth aspiny dendritic tree, a 40 millimetre local axon, and the ability to hold a millisecond-accurate beat for minutes.

CHAPTER 47. CHOLECYSTOKININ BASKET CELLS (Mood-Swayed Loose-Timing Silencing Neuron) (COG)

This is the only neuron in your brain that can be told to shut up by the very cell it is inhibiting, and it is also the cell that cannabis switches off.

Cholecystokinin is a gut peptide borrowed as a stain and tells a student nothing about hippocampal work.

Mood-Swayed names the cell's defining vulnerability, because its terminals carry cannabinoid type one receptors, so an active pyramidal cell can release endocannabinoids backwards and switch this inhibition off for seconds, and the same terminals are steered by serotonin, acetylcholine and stress signals.

Silencing names the shared job with the precise sibling above, and the contrast between Clockwork-Precise and Mood-Swayed is the whole point, since these cells deliver slow, asynchronous, unreliable inhibition that reports internal state rather than time.

Neuron marks it as an inhibitory local cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Like the parvalbumin basket cell this is an inhibitory interneuron that wraps baskets of endings around the cell bodies of pyramidal and granule cells, but it is named for cholecystokinin (CCK), a peptide better known for telling the gallbladder to contract and, in the brain, for raising anxiety.

If the parvalbumin cell is the metronome, this one is the volume knob and the mood dial, delivering slower and less reliable inhibition that tracks the emotional state of the animal and that the target cell can switch off on demand.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A round to spindle-shaped cell body at the border of the pyramidal cell layer, with sparsely spiny vertical dendrites reaching up into stratum lacunosum-moleculare to collect entorhinal and subcortical input, and a looser, less densely branched perisomatic basket than its parvalbumin cousin.

The terminals carry type 1 cannabinoid (CB1) receptors at the highest density found anywhere in the mammalian brain.

SIZE:

The cell body is 15 to 25 micrometres across, dendrites span 400 to 800 micrometres, and the axon totals several millimetres with thousands of boutons spread over an area comparable to that of a parvalbumin cell.

Basket cell boutons carry on average about 78 percent more CB1 protein than the boutons of cannabinoid-sensitive cells that target dendrites instead.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

There are at least two chemical flavours, one containing vasoactive intestinal polypeptide and one containing vesicular glutamate transporter 3, and the second can package glutamate as well as gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) and so release an excitatory and an inhibitory signal from the same terminal.

Cholecystokinin cells that target dendrites rather than cell bodies form a separate class not counted here.

WHERE IT LIVES:

At the stratum pyramidale and stratum radiatum border of CA1, CA2 and CA3, in the granule cell layer and molecular layer of the dentate gyrus, in the subiculum and in the superficial layers of the entorhinal cortex.

Sitting on that interface is functional, because it lets the cell keep its dendrites in the incoming cortical input while its axon works on the cell bodies below.

NUMBER:

From about 15 million hippocampal formation interneurons across both hemispheres, cholecystokinin cells are commonly put at 10 to 15 percent of interneurons with basket cells the majority of those, giving roughly 1.0 to 2.0 million across both hemispheres.

That is about 1 percent of the hippocampal formation and about 0.0017 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons of the nervous system, at fair confidence, though one anchoring measurement is solid: in CA1 the great majority of basket cells contain cholecystokinin and almost every such cell carries CB1 receptors.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Regular-spiking and accommodating, the opposite of its neighbour, with broad action potentials 0.6 to 1.0 milliseconds wide, a high input resistance of 150 to 300 megaohms, a long membrane time constant of 15 to 25 milliseconds and maximum rates in the tens rather than the hundreds of spikes per second.

In a behaving animal these cells prefer the ascending phase of the theta rhythm, roughly opposite to parvalbumin basket cells, so the two perisomatic systems take turns.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs come from local pyramidal cells, from entorhinal afferents on the distal dendrites, and distinctively from subcortical modulators, since these cells uniquely express the 5-HT3A receptor, an ion channel opened directly by serotonin, and are strongly driven by acetylcholine through muscarinic receptors.

Their output synapses use GABA-A receptors enriched in the alpha2 subunit, the subunit through which benzodiazepines calm rather than sedate, and release is triggered by N-type calcium channels sitting further from the release machinery than the P/Q-type channels of parvalbumin cells, which makes transmission unreliable, variable and partly asynchronous.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

A locally branching, unmyelinated axon a few millimetres long, thin and slow, conducting at well under a metre per second, so its inhibition arrives with a spread of delays rather than all at once.

That is precisely why it cannot generate a fast rhythm and instead sets a slowly varying background level.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

GABA is the primary transmitter, cholecystokinin is co-released during high-frequency firing and diffuses away to act on CCK2 receptors over seconds, and the vesicular glutamate transporter 3 subtype also releases glutamate.

The cell is switched off by endocannabinoids from its target and directly excited by serotonin and acetylcholine.

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable in the usual sense, and the reason matters: the axon is unmyelinated because the cell's entire design is built around slow, imprecise, heavily modulated inhibition, so no oligodendrocyte wraps it.

Stating this plainly prevents a common modelling error, because anyone who myelinates this axon by default destroys the property that defines the cell.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Moderate and far below those of the parvalbumin basket cell, since firing rates are an order of magnitude lower and mitochondrial density is unremarkable, which also makes these cells far less vulnerable to oxidative stress.

Peptide synthesis adds a modest cost, because cholecystokinin must be made in the cell body and shipped down the axon in dense-core vesicles that cannot be recycled locally.

PLASTICITY:

The signature is depolarisation-induced suppression of inhibition, a backwards message: when the target pyramidal cell fires hard, calcium entering it triggers synthesis of the endocannabinoid 2-arachidonoylglycerol, which drifts back across the synapse, binds CB1 receptors, closes N-type calcium channels and silences release for tens of seconds.

Blocking CB1 with the drug AM251 raises action-potential-evoked calcium in these boutons by about 54 percent, showing the brake is applied continuously even at rest.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

There is no perineuronal net here, and that absence is the specialisation, leaving the cell structurally plastic throughout life while its parvalbumin neighbour is locked down.

Astrocytes join the endocannabinoid loop directly, because they carry CB1 receptors too and answer the same 2-arachidonoylglycerol cloud with calcium waves and glutamate release, spreading one pyramidal cell's decision to neighbouring synapses; microglia here are unremarkable.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove cholecystokinin basket cells and the hippocampus keeps perfect time but loses all sense of mood and context, because inhibition becomes a rigid clock that no experience can adjust.

Serotonin and acetylcholine would lose their fastest route into perisomatic control, anxiety would no longer modulate encoding, and neither the brain's own endocannabinoids nor cannabis itself would have any perisomatic effect at all.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Retrograde signalling is the headline and the clearest case in the brain of a message travelling backwards across a synapse, while asynchronous release means the terminal is not a digital device, since transmitter keeps leaking out after the spike in proportion to residual calcium and deliberately blurs timing.

Peptide release by volume transmission changes the excitability of a whole neighbourhood for seconds, the cells are coupled to one another by both chemical synapses and gap junctions, and endocannabinoid release modulates the electrical coupling as well as the chemical transmission.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Excitation from local pyramidal cells and entorhinal afferents, 2000 to 6000 synapses per cell.

◦ Serotonin acting on ionotropic 5-HT3A receptors within milliseconds, scalar 0 to 1, and acetylcholine on muscarinic receptors, scalar 0 to 1.

◦ Endocannabinoid concentration at the terminal, scalar 0 to 1, arriving from the postsynaptic target.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Slow variable GABA-A inhibition onto somata and proximal dendrites, alpha2 enriched, decay 8 to 20 milliseconds.

◦ Cholecystokinin peptide into the extracellular space during high-frequency firing, and glutamate from the transporter-3 subtype.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts and CB1 receptor occupancy 0 to 1.

◦ Residual presynaptic calcium driving asynchronous release, and peptide store 0 to 1.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rest minus 62 millivolts, threshold minus 45 millivolts, spike width 0.6 to 1.0 milliseconds.

◦ Input resistance 150 to 300 megaohms, membrane time constant 15 to 25 milliseconds, baseline release probability 0.4 to 0.5.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 20 milliseconds, synchronous release under 2 milliseconds, asynchronous tail 20 to 60 milliseconds, endocannabinoid suppression 5 to 20 seconds, peptide action seconds to minutes.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Cannabis floods CB1 receptors and silences these terminals for hours instead of seconds, producing the failure of hippocampal encoding users experience as short-term memory loss.

◦ Dysfunction here is implicated in anxiety disorders and in the action of antidepressants, which work partly through cholecystokinin neurons of the dentate gyrus.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to reproduce depolarisation induced suppression of inhibition, in which a pyramidal cell silences the cholecystokinin basket cell that inhibits it.
Create a number variable called endocannabinoid with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called residual calcium with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called release count with initial value zero.
Define a function called advance one millisecond that performs the following operations.
    Set endocannabinoid to the product of endocannabinoid and nine hundred and ninety nine thousandths.
    Set residual calcium to the product of residual calcium and nine hundred and seventy five thousandths.
    Set release probability to the product of forty five hundredths and the difference of one and endocannabinoid.
    Check if a presynaptic spike arrives this millisecond.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Check if a random number between zero and one is less than release probability.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set release count to the sum of release count and one.
        End the conditional block.
        Set residual calcium to the sum of residual calcium and release probability.
    End the conditional block.
    Check if a postsynaptic spike arrives this millisecond.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set endocannabinoid to the sum of endocannabinoid and nine hundredths.
    End the conditional block.
    Return release count to the calling function.
End the function definition.
Repeat the following steps exactly two thousand times.
    Call the function named advance one millisecond.
End the loop.
Display release count to the console.
Set release count to zero.
Repeat the following steps exactly three thousand times.
    Call the function named advance one millisecond.
End the loop.
Display release count to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

// JavaScript is chosen because this is a message passing loop between two cells,
// and a short event driven script shows the backward signal most clearly.
const DT = 1.0;              // milliseconds per step
const TAU_ECB = 6000.0;      // endocannabinoid clearance, milliseconds
const P0 = 0.45;             // baseline release probability per presynaptic spike
const ECB_PER_SPIKE = 0.09;  // endocannabinoid made by one postsynaptic spike
const ASYNC_TAU = 40.0;      // decay of residual presynaptic calcium
let ecb = 0.0, residual = 0.0;
function step(preSpike, postSpike) {
  ecb -= (ecb / TAU_ECB) * DT;
  residual -= (residual / ASYNC_TAU) * DT;
  if (postSpike) ecb = Math.min(1.0, ecb + ECB_PER_SPIKE);
  const p = P0 * (1.0 - ecb);                  // CB1 closes the N type channels
  let quanta = 0;
  if (preSpike) {
    if (Math.random() < p) quanta += 1;        // synchronous release, on time
    residual += p;                             // and a residue that leaks later
  }
  if (Math.random() < residual * 0.02 * DT) quanta += 1;   // asynchronous release
  return quanta;
}
let before = 0, after = 0;
for (let t = 0; t < 2000; t += DT) before += step(t % 20 === 0, false);
for (let t = 0; t < 1000; t += DT) step(false, t % 10 === 0);   // depolarise target
for (let t = 0; t < 2000; t += DT) after += step(t % 20 === 0, false);
console.log("quanta in 2 s of 50 Hz input, before:", before, "after:", after);
console.log("suppression:", Math.round(100 * (1 - after / before)) + " percent");

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Depolarise the pyramidal cell it inhibits for a few seconds and the inhibition vanishes for ten to twenty seconds and then slowly returns, which no other perisomatic inhibitory cell in the hippocampus will do.

Add broad accommodating spikes, a high input resistance, direct excitation by serotonin, and terminals carrying more CB1 receptor than anywhere else in the brain, and the identification is certain.

CHAPTER 48. AXO-AXONIC CELLS (Spike-Gate Veto Neuron) (COG)

Every other neuron in the brain argues about whether a cell should fire; this one puts its hand directly over the trigger.

Spike names the single event this cell controls, because it places every one of its synapses on the axon initial segment - the short stretch of membrane where a pyramidal cell's action potential is actually born.

Gate names the position, since nothing the dendrites compute can leave the cell without passing that point.

Veto names the power, because roughly sixteen inhibitory boutons converge on one initial segment from about four of these cells, and in the intact animal they fire at the peak of the theta rhythm, precisely when the pyramidal cells they guard are least likely to speak.

Neuron marks it as a local circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The axo-axonic cell, also called the chandelier cell, is an inhibitory interneuron with one obsession: it makes synapses on the axon initial segment of principal cells, the short stretch of axon just past the cell body where the action potential is actually born, and on nothing else.

By owning that one square micrometre of membrane it can veto the output of a pyramidal cell however strongly the rest of the cell has been excited, and a single such cell holds that veto over more than a thousand principal cells.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

An ovoid or spindle-shaped cell body in or just below the principal cell layer, with smooth dendrites radiating into the layers above and below, and an axon that resolves into cartridges, short vertical strings of two to ten boutons each climbing the initial segment of one target cell, which under the microscope look like the candles of a chandelier.

The cell is parvalbumin-positive and usually wrapped in a perineuronal net.

SIZE:

The cell body is 15 to 25 micrometres across and dendrites span 500 to 800 micrometres.

A single reconstructed chandelier cell in CA1 was estimated to contact 1214 pyramidal cells, counted by tallying every distinct cartridge, and because each cartridge holds only a handful of synapses the total bouton count is in the thousands rather than the tens of thousands.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

CA1 has at least two positional variants, one with cell bodies in stratum pyramidale and a horizontally oriented type in stratum oriens, while dentate gyrus axo-axonic cells target granule cell initial segments and are notable for becoming functionally isolated from their inputs after experimental status epilepticus.

Entorhinal and subicular axo-axonic cells follow the same rule of exclusive initial segment targeting.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Stratum pyramidale and stratum oriens of CA1, CA2 and CA3, the granule cell layer and subgranular zone of the dentate gyrus, the subicular pyramidal layer, and layers II and III of the entorhinal cortex.

The decisive location is not where the cell body sits but where the terminals go, which is the interface between the cell body and the axon of every principal neuron within reach.

NUMBER:

From about 15 million hippocampal formation interneurons across both hemispheres, axo-axonic cells are consistently reported as one of the rarer classes at roughly 2 to 5 percent of interneurons, giving 0.3 to 0.75 million across both hemispheres.

That is around 0.4 percent of the hippocampal formation and about 0.0006 percent of the roughly 86 billion neurons of the nervous system, at low to fair confidence, and the striking figure is the leverage: half a million cells holding a veto over more than a thousand principal cells each.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Fast-spiking like the parvalbumin basket cell, with brief spikes, a large fast afterhyperpolarisation, sustained rates well above 100 per second, an input resistance of 60 to 150 megaohms and a membrane time constant of 8 to 15 milliseconds.

Their behaviour in a living animal is what sets them apart, because they are strongly theta modulated and fire on the descending phase just after the theta peak, earlier than basket cells, and then fall completely silent during sharp-wave ripples at the precise moment pyramidal cells fire hardest, so the withdrawal of inhibition from the initial segment is part of what allows memory replay to happen.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs come from local pyramidal cells, from CA3 and entorhinal afferents, from the medial septum, and from long-range inhibitory projections that appear responsible for the silencing during ripples.

Every output synapse lands on an axon initial segment and none anywhere else, using GABA-A receptors enriched in the alpha2 subunit, with only two to ten synapses per target, which is enough because the initial segment has the lowest voltage threshold and the highest sodium channel density in the whole neuron.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

A locally branching, largely unmyelinated axon a few millimetres long, shorter than a parvalbumin basket cell's because the cell reaches each target with a handful of boutons rather than a dense wrap, and slow in absolute terms but crossing its small territory in a few milliseconds.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

GABA, plus parvalbumin as a calcium buffer, and the interesting fact is not what is released but what it does on arrival, because the initial segment handles chloride differently from the rest of the cell.

The transporter KCC2, which pumps chloride out and makes GABA inhibitory, is scarce in the axon while NKCC1, which pumps chloride in, is present, so chloride sits higher there and the GABA reversal potential at the initial segment is depolarised, reported around minus 55 millivolts against about minus 72 millivolts at the cell body.

MYELINATION:

The cell's own axon is largely unmyelinated, and what matters more is that its target sits immediately before the first myelin sheath of the pyramidal cell axon.

The pyramidal cell adjusts the length and position of its initial segment over hours in response to activity, and moving it away from the cell body creates a mismatch with the existing cartridges that weakens the veto, which is structural plasticity used as gain control.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

High for the same reason as the parvalbumin basket cell, because fast firing means heavy ion pumping and a dense mitochondrial complement, which leaves these cells vulnerable to metabolic and oxidative insult and among the first inhibitory neurons lost in chronic epilepsy.

PLASTICITY:

These synapses are plastic in an unusual way because the postsynaptic structure itself moves, as the initial segment lengthens, shortens and shifts along the axon and the boutons must follow or lose contact.

The chloride gradient inside the initial segment is also adjustable, so the same synapse can be more or less inhibitory as transporter expression changes with development, activity and disease.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Specialised astrocytic processes and oligodendrocyte precursor cells contact the axon initial segment region directly, and because this synapse depends on a local chloride gradient rather than a global one, the ability of nearby glia to buffer potassium and shape chloride handling in that tiny microdomain matters far more here than anywhere else in the circuit.

The perineuronal net around the cell body plays the same protective and stabilising role as it does for parvalbumin basket cells.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove axo-axonic cells and the hippocampus loses its last-moment gate: basket cells would still shape the rhythm, but nothing could override a strongly excited pyramidal cell at the final step.

The clean alternation in which inhibition is applied to the initial segment during exploration and lifted during replay would disappear, and memory consolidation during rest would lose its timing signal.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

This is the clearest case in the brain of a computation resting on a subcellular ion gradient rather than on wiring, because high chloride in the initial segment means the synapse depolarises its target while still opposing spike generation, clamping the membrane near the chloride reversal potential and shunting incoming current away, and in cortex it has been reported under some conditions to excite rather than inhibit, which remains genuinely contested.

The cell also instructs structural remodelling of its target's initial segment, is gap-junction coupled to other parvalbumin interneurons, is silenced by long-range inhibition during ripples so that its computation is set by brain state rather than local activity, and contributes ephaptic field effects to the very initial segments it contacts.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Excitation from local pyramidal cells, CA3 and entorhinal afferents, 3000 to 10000 synapses per cell.

◦ Long-range inhibition from the medial septum and other inhibitory projection neurons, which silences the cell during sharp-wave ripples.

◦ Brain state, categorical: exploration with theta, or rest with ripples.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ GABA-A conductance delivered only to the axon initial segments of 1000 to 1500 principal cells, two to ten synapses each.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane voltage in millivolts and preferred theta phase on the descending slope after the peak.

◦ Chloride concentration in each target initial segment, plus that segment's length and offset in micrometres.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Rest minus 65 millivolts, threshold minus 52 millivolts, refractory period 1 millisecond, sustained rate up to 150 per second.

◦ GABA reversal about minus 55 millivolts at the initial segment against about minus 72 millivolts at the soma, peak inhibitory conductance about twice the resting leak.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 10 milliseconds, synaptic decay 3 to 8 milliseconds, theta cycle 125 to 250 milliseconds, ripple episode 50 to 100 milliseconds, initial segment remodelling hours to days.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ In chronic temporal lobe epilepsy these cells are lost or disconnected from their inputs, removing the last-line veto on pyramidal output.

◦ If chloride rises further the synapse can flip from clamping the initial segment below threshold to pushing it above, turning the brake into an accelerator.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compare inhibition delivered to the axon initial segment with the same inhibition delivered to the cell body.
Define a function called count spikes that performs the following operations.
    Create a number variable called voltage with initial value minus sixty five.
    Create a number variable called spike total with initial value zero.
    Create a number variable called clock with initial value zero.
    While clock is less than one thousand, repeat the following steps.
        Set pull to the product of inhibitory conductance and the difference of reversal potential and voltage.
        Set change to the quotient of the sum of the sum of the difference of minus sixty five and voltage and excitatory drive and pull divided by twelve.
        Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the product of change and one hundredth.
        Check if voltage is greater than or equal to minus fifty two.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set voltage to minus sixty five.
            Set spike total to the sum of spike total and one.
        End the conditional block.
        Set clock to the sum of clock and one hundredth.
    End the loop.
    Return spike total to the calling function.
End the function definition.
Create a list variable called drive levels with initial value empty list.
Add ten to the end of drive levels.
Add twenty to the end of drive levels.
Add forty to the end of drive levels.
Add sixty to the end of drive levels.
For each drive level in drive levels, perform the following operations.
    Set excitatory drive to drive level.
    Set inhibitory conductance to zero.
    Call the function named count spikes.
    Display the returned value to the console.
    Set inhibitory conductance to two.
    Set reversal potential to minus seventy two.
    Call the function named count spikes.
    Display the returned value to the console.
    Set reversal potential to minus fifty five.
    Call the function named count spikes.
    Display the returned value to the console.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

/* C is chosen because a whole brain simulator runs this inner loop billions of times, and C shows exactly what that loop costs with nothing hidden. */
#include <stdio.h>
#define DT 0.01            /* milliseconds per step */
#define TAU 12.0           /* time constant of the initial segment, ms */
#define VREST -65.0
#define VTH -52.0          /* the initial segment has the lowest threshold */
#define EGABA_AIS -55.0    /* depolarised, because KCC2 is scarce in the axon */
#define EGABA_SOMA -72.0   /* the cell body pumps chloride out */
static int spikes(double drive_mv, double g_inh, double e_gaba, double ms) {
    double v = VREST;
    int n = 0;
    long steps = (long)(ms / DT);
    for (long i = 0; i < steps; i++) {
        double dv = ((VREST - v) + drive_mv + g_inh * (e_gaba - v)) / TAU;
        v += dv * DT;
        if (v >= VTH) { v = VREST; n++; }      /* one output spike */
    }
    return n;
}
int main(void) {
    double drives[4] = { 10.0, 20.0, 40.0, 60.0 };   /* millivolts of excitation */
    printf("drive   none   somatic   axo-axonic\n");
    for (int k = 0; k < 4; k++) {
        printf("%5.0f %6d %9d %12d\n", drives[k],
               spikes(drives[k], 0.0, 0.0, 1000.0),
               spikes(drives[k], 2.0, EGABA_SOMA, 1000.0),
               spikes(drives[k], 2.0, EGABA_AIS, 1000.0));
    }
    return 0;
}
/* Somatic inhibition silences the cell until the drive is very large, while
   axo-axonic inhibition, with its depolarised chloride reversal, only raises the
   bar a little and lets strong inputs through. */

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Fill the cell with dye and look at the terminals: short vertical strings of boutons, each climbing a single axon initial segment, with nothing on any cell body or dendrite anywhere, a pattern no other neuron in the nervous system produces.

Record it in a behaving animal and watch it fire hard on the descending phase of theta and then go completely silent during a sharp-wave ripple, at the one moment when every pyramidal cell around it is firing.

CHAPTER 49. BISTRATIFIED CELLS (Inner-Dendrite Restraining Neuron) (COG)

Picture a gardener who prunes only the branches facing the road and leaves the crown untouched.

Inner-Dendrite names the territory, because this cell lays its axon across the proximal apical and basal dendrites - the belt where the fibres coming from area CA3 land.

Restraining names the work, which is to hold down excitation exactly where that CA3 traffic arrives rather than at the cell body.

The word also records its timing signature, since it carries both parvalbumin and somatostatin and delivers its strongest inhibition at the very start of a burst of network activity, then weakens as the rhythm repeats.

Neuron marks it as a local inhibitory circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The bistratified cell is an inhibitory neuron that lays its output in two flat sheets, above and below the row of CA1 pyramidal cells, exactly where fibres from area CA3 land on their dendrites.

A dendrite is the branching input cable a neuron listens with, so this cell mutes recall where it arrives and leaves entorhinal input untouched.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The cell body sits in stratum pyramidale, the dark band of packed pyramidal cell bodies, and both dendrites and axon fill stratum oriens and radiatum, stopping dead at stratum lacunosum-moleculare.

SIZE:

The cell body is 15 to 20 micrometres across in rodents and perhaps 18 to 25 in humans, while the axon totals tens of millimetres inside a rice-grain volume.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

No formal subtypes exist, though some weight their axon towards radiatum and others towards oriens, and versions occur in CA1, CA2 and CA3.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Stratum pyramidale and upper stratum oriens of hippocampal areas CA1, CA2 and CA3, with the axon in stratum oriens and stratum radiatum.

The defining interface is the sharp line between stratum radiatum and stratum lacunosum-moleculare, where CA3 input ends and entorhinal input begins, and this cell never crosses it.

NUMBER:

Rat CA1 holds an estimated 2,210 bistratified cells, 5.7 percent of its 38,500 interneurons, which scales to roughly 100,000 per human hemisphere and perhaps 250,000 across both.

Confidence is moderate because nobody has counted them in a human brain, and that is 0.17 percent of hippocampal formation neurons, or three cells in every million neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Nearly fast-spiking, with 100 to 200 megaohms input resistance, an 8 to 12 millisecond membrane time constant, half-millisecond spikes, firing near the theta trough and ferociously during ripples.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Driven by the very CA3 fibres it inhibits and by local pyramidal collaterals, it does feedforward and feedback inhibition at once, contacting about 1,469 pyramidal cells.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Very thin at 0.2 to 0.5 micrometres and hugely branched, with thousands of boutons, the swellings where transmitter is released, conducting near 0.4 metres per second.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's main quietening chemical, plus somatostatin and neuropeptide Y released only during hard firing; parvalbumin is a calcium sponge, not a transmitter.

MYELINATION:

Patchy and proximal only, since much hippocampal myelin, the fatty wrap that speeds conduction, sits on parvalbumin interneuron axons.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Among the highest of any neuron, dense with mitochondria and cytochrome c oxidase, leaving it fragile in oxygen shortage, ageing and schizophrenia.

PLASTICITY:

Its excitatory inputs strengthen without the cell firing, called anti-Hebbian plasticity, while its output synapses onto pyramidal cells weaken under natural activity.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Unusually it wears a perineuronal net, a stiff astrocyte-built lattice that buffers ions during furious firing; microglia chew holes in it during learning.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete it and CA1 can no longer say no to CA3 selectively, tilting the region towards remembering rather than perceiving.

Ripples survive but lose the dendritic clamp that stops them spreading into a seizure.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Gap junctions, direct pores passing current with no chemical step, weld these cells to each other and to basket cells so the parvalbumin network fires as one.

Neuropeptide Y drifts to receptors far from any synapse, a mode called volume transmission.

Its inhibition suppresses dendritic calcium spikes, the local events that trigger lasting change, giving it a veto over memory formation at particular branches.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: CA3 Schaffer collaterals, 0 to 100 per second; local pyramidal feedback, 0 to 50 per second; theta-locked septal GABA.

• OUTPUTS: GABA onto pyramidal dendritic shafts and spines in oriens and radiatum, about 1,469 targets; peptides above 40 hertz.

• STATE: membrane voltage, refractory counter, inhibitory conductance, peptide store, parvalbumin-bound calcium.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 65 millivolts; threshold minus 45 millivolts; 100 to 200 megaohms; divergence about 1,600 cells.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 8 to 12 milliseconds; inhibitory decay 4 to 8; theta cycle 100 to 200; ripple cycle 5 to 8.

• FAILURE MODE: loss frees CA3 drive and feeds temporal lobe epilepsy; parvalbumin failure is suspected in schizophrenia and in degraded Alzheimer ripples.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to decide each millisecond whether a bistratified cell fires and how much inhibition reaches proximal pyramidal dendrites.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value -65.
Create a number variable called inhibition with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called index with initial value 0.
While index is less than the total number of items in schaffer_trace, repeat the following steps.
    Set drive to the product of 1.8 and the item at position index in schaffer_trace.
    Set feedback to the product of 1.1 and the item at position index in pyramidal_trace.
    Set voltage to the sum of -65 and the product of the sum of voltage and 65 and 0.895.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the sum of drive and feedback.
    If voltage is greater than or equal to -45, then perform the following actions.
        Set voltage to -65.
        Set inhibition to the sum of inhibition and 0.6.
    End the conditional block.
    Set inhibition to the product of inhibition and 0.819.
    Set index to the sum of index and 1.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python is chosen because it runs anywhere with no libraries, so a reader can paste it in and watch the cell fire.

import math
DT, V_REST, V_TH = 1.0, -65.0, -45.0
MD, ID = math.exp(-DT / 9.0), math.exp(-DT / 5.0)   # membrane, inhibition decay

def bistratified(schaffer, feedback):
    v, g, refrac, out = V_REST, 0.0, 0, []
    for s, f in zip(schaffer, feedback):
        v = V_REST + (v - V_REST) * MD + 1.8 * s + 1.1 * f
        spike = 0
        if refrac > 0:                    # about 2 ms dead time
            refrac, v = refrac - 1, V_REST
        elif v >= V_TH:
            spike, v, refrac = 1, V_REST, 2
            g += 0.6                      # proximal dendritic inhibition
        g *= ID
        out.append((spike, round(g, 3)))
    return out

sc = [0.0] * 5 + [12.0] * 10 + [0.0] * 5  # ripple-like CA3 burst
print(bistratified(sc, [0.0] + sc[:-1]))  # feedback lags one step

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Two flat axon sheets straddling the pyramidal layer, stopping cleanly at stratum lacunosum-moleculare, parvalbumin and somatostatin and neuropeptide Y in one cell, and a ripple burst near 100 spikes per second arriving one to two milliseconds after the pyramidal cells.

CHAPTER 50. ORIENS-LACUNOSUM MOLECULARE (O-LM) CELLS (Outer-Dendrite Filtering Neuron) (COG)

This cell lies flat on the floor of the hippocampus, listens only to its neighbours, then reaches to the ceiling to switch off the outside world.

The old label is a pair of Latin layer addresses, one for the cell body and one for the axon, and it teaches nothing about purpose.

Outer-Dendrite names the target, the most distal tuft of the pyramidal cell - which is the only place the direct pathway from the entorhinal cortex makes contact.

Filtering names the job, because by damping that tuft while leaving the proximal dendrites free, this cell decides how much raw cortical sensory input is allowed to compete with the hippocampus's own stored CA3 traffic.

The word also reflects its timing, as its output is weak on the first stimulus and grows with repeated theta-frequency activation, so the filter tightens as a rhythm is sustained.

Neuron marks it as a local inhibitory circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The oriens-lacunosum moleculare cell, shortened to O-LM cell, keeps its body and listening branches in the deepest layer of CA1 and sends its output to the top layer, where the entorhinal cortex delivers news of the world.

It uses purely local chatter to decide how much reality its neighbours hear.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A horizontal spindle of a cell body sits in stratum oriens with two or three dendrites that never leave that layer, while one axon climbs the full height of CA1 into a tuft.

SIZE:

The cell body is 15 to 25 micrometres long, its dendrites reach 400 to 900 micrometres sideways, and the axon climbs 400 to 600.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Most are born in the medial ganglionic eminence and carry somatostatin, while a caudal ganglionic eminence minority carry the serotonin receptor 5-HT3A instead.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Stratum oriens of area CA1 against the alveus, with equivalent cells in CA3, and a terminal field beyond the radiatum to lacunosum-moleculare boundary, where CA3 input yields to entorhinal.

NUMBER:

Rat CA1 holds an estimated 1,640 O-LM cells, 4.3 percent of its interneurons, scaling to roughly 75,000 per human hemisphere and 150,000 in both, or 0.1 percent of hippocampal formation neurons.

Confidence is low, since no human count exists.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Slow and rhythmic, with 200 to 400 megaohms input resistance, a 20 to 40 millisecond membrane time constant, and a voltage sag marking the h-current that paces it near theta.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Nearly all excitation comes from local CA1 pyramidal collaterals, a pure feedback loop whose synapses grow stronger with every spike, called short-term facilitation.

The protein Elfn1 switches on metabotropic glutamate receptor 7 across the synapse, holding release probability down so only a burst recruits this cell.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

One thin ascending trunk of 0.3 to 0.6 micrometres taking milliseconds to reach the tuft, with some cells branching to subiculum or septum.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's principal quietening chemical, plus somatostatin co-released during sustained firing to damp excitation over seconds.

MYELINATION:

None, and the bare axon is deliberate, because this cell must inhibit the tuft on a rhythm, not instantly.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Moderate to high, because h-current channels leak positive charge at rest, so ion pumps run constantly and the cell pays a bill even when silent.

PLASTICITY:

Theta-frequency stimulation potentiates its inputs and its intrinsic excitability, the latter by turning down Kv7 potassium channels and h-channels.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

It wears no perineuronal net, unlike parvalbumin cells, and astrocytes wrap its terminals loosely enough for somatostatin to escape and act at range.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove it and CA1 cannot ignore the world on purpose, so every sensory detail pushes into recall and no memory holds steady against interference.

CA1 theta also loses a main pacemaker.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The h-current makes this a resonator rather than an adder, tuned to theta and deaf to other rates, which is computation done by the membrane itself.

Glutamate and acetylcholine together trigger plateau potentials, shoulders lasting hundreds of milliseconds that turn a brief input into a sustained output.

Its inhibition blocks the tuft calcium spikes that let entorhinal and CA3 input combine, vetoing the coincidence detection that builds associations.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: local CA1 pyramidal collaterals, strongly facilitating, 0 to 60 hertz; septal acetylcholine; theta-locked septal GABA.

• OUTPUTS: GABA onto the distal apical tufts of about 1,457 CA1 pyramidal cells; somatostatin above 20 hertz.

• STATE: membrane voltage, h-current activation, facilitation factor, afterhyperpolarisation, somatostatin store.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 55 to minus 62 millivolts; threshold minus 45; 200 to 400 megaohms; sag ratio 1.2 to 1.5.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 20 to 40 milliseconds; h-current 100 to 200; facilitation 150 to 300.

• FAILURE MODE: somatostatin interneurons are lost early and selectively in Alzheimer's disease, and fewer of them tracks with faster cognitive decline and degraded theta.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to recruit an O-LM cell only when local pyramidal cells fire a sustained burst, then inhibit the distal tuft.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value -55.
Create a number variable called facilitation with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called tuft_inhibition with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called index with initial value 0.
While index is less than the total number of items in pyramidal_trace, repeat the following steps.
    Set arriving to the item at position index in pyramidal_trace.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the quotient of the difference of -55 and voltage divided by 20.
    If arriving is equal to 1, then perform the following actions.
        Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the product of 2 and facilitation.
        Set facilitation to the sum of facilitation and 0.8.
    End the conditional block.
    Set facilitation to the sum of facilitation and the quotient of the difference of 1 and facilitation divided by 200.
    If voltage is greater than or equal to -45, then perform the following actions.
