        Set voltage to -63.
        Set tuft_inhibition to the sum of tuft_inhibition and 0.5.
    End the conditional block.
    Set tuft_inhibition to the product of tuft_inhibition and 0.95.
    Set index to the sum of index and 1.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python again, because the sag current needs a real exponential and one changed number shifts the tuning.

import math
V_REST, V_TH = -60.0, -45.0
G_H, E_H, W = 0.35, -20.0, 2.0   # sag conductance, its reversal, synaptic weight

def olm(pyr_spikes, steps=800):
    v, h, f, out = V_REST, 0.0, 1.0, []
    for t in range(steps):
        h_inf = 1.0 / (1.0 + math.exp((v + 80.0) / 6.0))  # sag grows when negative
        h += (h_inf - h) / 120.0
        drive = 0.0
        if t in pyr_spikes:
            drive = W * f                 # Elfn1 and mGluR7: each spike lands harder
            f = min(4.0, f + 0.8)
        f += (1.0 - f) / 200.0
        v += (V_REST - v) / 25.0 + G_H * h * (E_H - v) + drive
        if v >= V_TH:
            out.append(t)
            v = V_REST - 8.0              # deep afterhyperpolarisation
    return out

print("first O-LM spikes:", olm({100 + 20 * i for i in range(30)})[:5])

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A horizontal spindle in stratum oriens whose dendrites never leave it, one axon climbing the full height of CA1, a pronounced sag, and an input that strengthens with every spike.

CHAPTER 51. IVY CELLS (COG)

The commonest inhibitory cell in the hippocampus went undescribed until 2008, because its axon is so fine that microscopists mistook it for haze.

Gloss: dense creeping axon that bathes nearby dendrites in slow inhibition.

Ivy is a plain shape word that teaches, naming the extremely dense, fine, creeping axonal plexus that clings around local pyramidal dendrites.

This chapter merges the two entries that previously described the ivy cell, one of which had been filed with the cortical interneurons although the cell is a hippocampal one.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The ivy cell is named for an axon that creeps through the tissue like ivy over a wall, thin and dense and everywhere.

Rather than striking a few targets hard it releases gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's main quietening chemical, as a slow mist over a neighbourhood, and makes the gas nitric oxide.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A small round body usually sits inside stratum pyramidale, with smooth dendrites through oriens, pyramidale and radiatum, and a fog of ultrafine axon filling the same layers.

SIZE:

The body is 10 to 15 micrometres across, the dendritic field spans 300 to 500 micrometres, and the axon reaches tens of millimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Cells vary by the layer holding the body, classically stratum pyramidale and a variant in stratum radiatum, and they shade into neurogliaform cells.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Stratum pyramidale, oriens and radiatum of areas CA1, CA2 and CA3, with equivalents in the dentate gyrus.

The relevant interface is the radiatum to lacunosum-moleculare border, which this cell will not cross, since beyond it is neurogliaform territory.

NUMBER:

Rat CA1 holds an estimated 8,810 ivy cells, 22.9 percent of its interneurons and the most numerous single type, scaling to about 390,000 per human hemisphere and 800,000 across both.

Confidence is good for the proportion, poor for the absolute figure, and that is 0.5 percent of hippocampal formation neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Late spiking, hesitating tens or hundreds of milliseconds before the first spike as a potassium brake lets go, with resistance above 250 megaohms and rates of one or two hertz.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Excited by CA3 Schaffer collaterals and local pyramidal cells, it slowly inhibits about 1,490 pyramidal cells, and mu-opioid receptors let opioids silence it.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Among the thinnest in the brain at under 0.2 micrometres, unmyelinated, and many release sites face no partner, the fingerprint of transmission by diffusion.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) in a dilute cloud, neuropeptide Y that suppresses nearby glutamate release, and nitric oxide made on demand.

MYELINATION:

None, since the axon is too thin for a sheath, and myelin would defeat a strategy needing bare membrane throughout.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Modest electrically but chemically distinctive, since making nitric oxide consumes arginine and a cofactor whose shortage flips the enzyme into making superoxide.

PLASTICITY:

Its diffuse output is reset wholesale by opioid signalling rather than synaptic learning, and its nitric oxide crosses other synapses backwards to change their output.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytes matter more here than almost anywhere, because their GABA transporters set how far the cloud spreads.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove ivy cells and CA1 loses its background hush, so events stay correctly timed while excitability drifts upward.

It also loses the link between activity, blood flow and opioid state.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Volume transmission is the headline, with GABA spilling into the extracellular space to reach cells having no synapse with it.

Gap junctions, direct electrical pores, link ivy cells promiscuously to neurogliaform and other interneurons, forming a loosely coupled sheet.

Nitric oxide is a retrograde messenger, a vasodilator and an immune signal at once, making this neuron part of the blood flow response brain scanners measure.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: CA3 Schaffer collaterals, 0 to 40 hertz; pyramidal feedback; mu-opioid agonists hyperpolarising 3 to 10 millivolts; gap junction current.

• OUTPUTS: slow extrasynaptic GABA over a 20 to 60 micrometre radius; neuropeptide Y; nitric oxide reaching vessels.

• STATE: membrane voltage, potassium brake, pooled GABA, nitric oxide, opioid occupancy.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 65 to minus 72 millivolts; 250 to 500 megaohms; first-spike delay 50 to 300 milliseconds.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 30 to 50 milliseconds; brake 100 to 200; pooled GABA 50 to 150; nitric oxide hundreds.

• FAILURE MODE: opioids silence these cells and strip out diffuse inhibition, and failed nitric oxide signalling uncouples activity from blood flow in dementia.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to simulate an ivy cell that hesitates before its first spike and then releases a slow cloud of gamma-aminobutyric acid and nitric oxide.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value -70.
Create a number variable called brake with initial value 0.9.
Create a number variable called gaba_cloud with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called nitric_oxide with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called index with initial value 0.
While index is less than the total number of items in drive_trace, repeat the following steps.
    Set drive to the item at position index in drive_trace.
    Set brake to the difference of brake and the quotient of brake divided by 120.
    Set pull to the product of brake and the difference of -85 and voltage.
    Set change to the quotient of the sum of pull and the difference of -70 and voltage divided by 45.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and the sum of change and drive.
    If voltage is greater than or equal to -50, then perform the following actions.
        Set voltage to -70.
        Set gaba_cloud to the sum of gaba_cloud and 1.
        Set nitric_oxide to the sum of nitric_oxide and 1.
    End the conditional block.
    Set gaba_cloud to the product of gaba_cloud and 0.989.
    Set nitric_oxide to the product of nitric_oxide and 0.9975.
    Set index to the sum of index and 1.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python, because the late-spiking delay is the whole point and a reader can print it and watch it move.

import math
V_REST, V_TH, E_K = -70.0, -50.0, -85.0
gd, v, gaba, no, first = 0.9, V_REST, 0.0, 0.0, None
for t, d in enumerate([0.0] * 100 + [0.8] * 1400):   # current from 100 ms on
    if d > 0:
        gd -= gd / 120.0            # the potassium brake lets go slowly
    v += (V_REST - v) / 45.0 + gd * (E_K - v) / 45.0 + d
    if v >= V_TH:
        v, gaba, no = V_REST, gaba + 1.0, no + 1.0   # puff of GABA and gas
        if first is None:
            first = t
    gaba *= math.exp(-1 / 90.0)
    no *= math.exp(-1 / 400.0)
print("first spike ms:", first, "delay:", first - 100)
print("GABA cloud:", round(gaba, 3), "nitric oxide:", round(no, 3))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A small body among the pyramidal cells inside a smudge of axon too fine to resolve, staining for nitric oxide synthase with neuropeptide Y, and that long hesitation before the first spike.

CHAPTER 52. HIPPOCAMPAL NEUROGLIAFORM CELLS (COG)

One spike from this cell fogs a sphere of tissue, and it alone can switch on the slow, seconds-long class of inhibition.

Gloss: the small round soma with its tight halo of thin branches is exactly what the name says.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The neurogliaform cell has an axon so densely tangled that early anatomists took it for a glial support cell, hence the name.

It aims at nothing in particular, flooding a small volume with gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's quietening chemical, which lingers long enough to reach a slow receptor class.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A small round body sends short smooth dendrites out symmetrically, while the axon branches repeatedly into hundreds of branchlets filling a sphere.

SIZE:

The body is 10 to 15 micrometres, the axonal sphere is 100 to 300 micrometres wide, and millimetres of axon pack inside.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

They divide by layer into a lacunosum-moleculare group marked by neuron-derived neurotrophic factor, a radiatum group and a dentate group, with a nitric oxide synthase subset shading into ivy.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Stratum lacunosum-moleculare and stratum radiatum of areas CA1, CA2 and CA3, and the molecular layer of the dentate gyrus.

The lacunosum-moleculare group sits at the interface where entorhinal layer 3 fibres, the temporoammonic path, enter the hippocampus, where the world meets retrieved memory.

NUMBER:

Rat CA1 holds an estimated 3,580 neurogliaform cells, 9.3 percent of its interneurons, and with dentate and CA3 groups perhaps 250,000 per human hemisphere, 500,000 in both.

Confidence is low for the absolute figure, which is 0.33 percent of hippocampal formation neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Late spiking with a first-spike delay over 100 milliseconds, 200 to 400 megaohms, and strong accommodation, so it reports onset rather than duration.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Driven by entorhinal fibres in lacunosum-moleculare or CA3 fibres in radiatum, it produces a unitary inhibitory current rising in 2.8 and decaying with a 51 millisecond time constant.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Ultrathin and unmyelinated with the shortest inter-bouton distances in the hippocampus, and so compact that conduction delay across the output field stays under a millisecond.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA) released as a prolonged low-concentration transient rather than a pulse, plus neuropeptide Y, and that slow transient is the heart of the cell.

MYELINATION:

None, and impossible at this calibre, and self-defeating anyway, because the strategy needs GABA escaping from bare membrane.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Modest given low rates and small size, the distinctive cost being upkeep of thousands of release sites in a tiny arbour.

PLASTICITY:

Its own synapses are not classic potentiation sites, but by gating whether entorhinal and CA3 input coincide in the tuft it controls plasticity in other cells.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Astrocytic GABA transporters decide how long the cloud persists, so blocking them measurably expands this cell's sphere of influence.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove it and the hippocampus loses slow inhibition entirely, so fast cells still shape each moment but nothing imposes a hush after entorhinal input.

The region could react but never settle.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Volume transmission is the operating principle, since this cell speaks chemically to a region rather than electrically to partners.

It is the only interneuron known to evoke a metabotropic response from a single spike, and desensitisation shapes that response.

Gap junction coupling is promiscuous, about 85 percent of pairs connected, so the population behaves partly as one element.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: entorhinal layer 3 or CA3 excitation, 0 to 30 hertz; gap junction current; opioids.

• OUTPUTS: a biphasic inhibitory conductance to every cell within 50 to 150 micrometres; neuropeptide Y.

• STATE: membrane voltage, potassium brake, extracellular GABA, fast conductance, metabotropic conductance, desensitisation.

• PARAMETERS: 200 to 400 megaohms; first-spike delay 50 to 200 milliseconds; rise 2.8 and decay 51; metabotropic peak one fifth.

• TIME CONSTANTS: fast rise 2.8 and decay 51 milliseconds; metabotropic rise 45 and decay 180; astrocytic clearance 50 to 200.

• FAILURE MODE: losing slow inhibition at the entorhinal input zone releases the direct sensory pathway into CA1, implicated in temporal lobe epilepsy, and opioids suppress these cells.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn one neurogliaform spike into the two slow inhibitory components it delivers to every cell inside its axonal sphere.
Create a number variable called fast_decay with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called fast_rise with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called slow_decay with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called slow_rise with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called index with initial value 0.
Create a list variable called output with initial value empty list.
While index is less than 400, repeat the following steps.
    If index is equal to 50, then perform the following actions.
        Set fast_decay to 1.
        Set fast_rise to 1.
        Set slow_decay to 1.
        Set slow_rise to 1.
    End the conditional block.
    Set fast_part to the product of 1.2525 and the difference of fast_decay and fast_rise.
    Set slow_part to the product of 0.4232 and the difference of slow_decay and slow_rise.
    Add the sum of fast_part and slow_part to the end of output.
    Set fast_decay to the product of fast_decay and 0.98058.
    Set fast_rise to the product of fast_rise and 0.69907.
    Set slow_decay to the product of slow_decay and 0.99446.
    Set slow_rise to the product of slow_rise and 0.97800.
    Set index to the sum of index and 1.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

C is chosen because the two-receptor kinetics are pure arithmetic and this loop runs billions of times in a whole-hippocampus simulation.

#include <stdio.h>
#include <math.h>
/* peak-normalise a difference of two exponentials */
static double nf(double td, double tr) {
    double tp = (td * tr / (td - tr)) * log(td / tr);
    return 1.0 / (exp(-tp / td) - exp(-tp / tr));
}
int main(void) {
    double aD = 0, aR = 0, bD = 0, bR = 0;
    double nA = nf(51.0, 2.8), nB = nf(180.0, 45.0);
    for (int t = 0; t < 400; t++) {
        if (t == 50) { aD++; aR++; bD++; bR++; }  /* one spike */
        double gA = nA * (aD - aR);               /* fast component */
        double gB = 0.2 * nB * (bD - bR);         /* metabotropic */
        if (t % 40 == 0) printf("%3d %.4f %.4f\n", t, gA, gB);
        aD *= exp(-1.0 / 51.0);  aR *= exp(-1.0 / 2.8);
        bD *= exp(-1.0 / 180.0); bR *= exp(-1.0 / 45.0);
    }
    return 0;
}

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A tiny body inside an axon fog like a cotton ball drawn in pencil, one spike giving two-part inhibition lasting hundreds of milliseconds, and neighbours coupled 85 percent of the time.

CHAPTER 53. HILAR PERFORANT PATH-ASSOCIATED (HIPP) CELLS (Granule-Return Muting Neuron) (COG)

This is the most fragile important cell in the hippocampus, and when temporal lobe epilepsy begins it dies first.

The old acronym encodes two addresses - the hilus where the body sits and the incoming path its axon runs beside - and cannot be spoken aloud usefully.

Granule-Return names the circuit loop, because the cell is excited by the very granule cells it then inhibits, making it a feedback element rather than an advance guard.

Muting names the effect and its place, since its somatostatin-containing axon spreads through the outer two thirds of the molecular layer - the band where cortical fibres contact granule cell dendrites - so the more the dentate fires, the more its own incoming line is turned down.

Neuron marks it as a local inhibitory circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The hilar perforant path-associated cell, the HIPP cell, sits in the hilus, the hollow inside the curve of the dentate gyrus, and sends its axon to the outer molecular layer.

That is where the perforant path, carrying the world in from entorhinal cortex, lands on granule cells, making this a feedback thermostat on memory's front door.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

A medium multipolar body lies in the hilus with thorny dendrites that never leave it, while the axon crosses the granule cell layer into the outer two thirds of the molecular layer.

SIZE:

The body is 15 to 25 micrometres in rodents and perhaps 20 to 30 in humans, dendrites reach 300 to 700 micrometres, and the axon field can span a millimetre.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Two contrasting somatostatin types exist, the classic HIPP cell with molecular layer output and a second whose output stays in the hilus, and a subset reaches the septum.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The hilus, or polymorphic layer, of the dentate gyrus, with the axon in the outer molecular layer.

Two interfaces matter, the hilus to granule layer border its axon crosses and its dendrites never do, and the line dividing mossy from perforant input.

NUMBER:

Rat hilar somatostatin neurons number in the low tens of thousands per hippocampus, 16 percent of dentate inhibitory cells, scaling to perhaps 100,000 to 250,000 per hemisphere.

Confidence is low and nobody has counted these in humans, so treat the midpoint, 0.2 percent of hippocampal formation neurons, as a bound.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Slow and regular, with 200 to 400 megaohms, a 25 to 40 millisecond membrane time constant, broad spikes with a large afterhyperpolarisation, and firing that rises when the animal explores.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Granule cell mossy fibre collaterals drive it through facilitating synapses, so only converging bursts recruit it, and it inhibits granule cell distal dendrites and basket cells.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Thin and unmyelinated, branching widely in the outer molecular layer and running unusually far along it.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the quietening chemical, with somatostatin and neuropeptide Y co-released to suppress the perforant path terminals.

MYELINATION:

Absent locally, though the long branch to the medial septum is partly myelinated because it must cross centimetres fast.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Moderate but brittle, since these cells sit in poorly capillarised tissue and buffer calcium badly, so hard excitation loads them fatally.

PLASTICITY:

The granule cell to HIPP synapse potentiates, and blocking that impairs object location memory, a rare link from one inhibitory synapse to a behaviour.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Its peptides act on stem cells in the subgranular zone next door, so it helps set the rate of adult neurogenesis, and microglia inflame at its death.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove HIPP cells and the dentate gyrus stops separating patterns, the trick of turning overlapping inputs into sparse distinct outputs that keeps similar memories apart.

What it loses is discrimination itself.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Somatostatin and neuropeptide Y diffuse onto perforant path terminals, volume transmission outlasting any spike by seconds.

Neuropeptide Y is one of the brain's own anticonvulsants, and surviving cells sprout it into new territory in human epileptic tissue.

The facilitating input makes this a burst detector, integrating at the synapse before the membrane is involved.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: granule cell mossy fibre collaterals, facilitating, 0 to 40 hertz with wide convergence; septal acetylcholine.

• OUTPUTS: GABA across the outer molecular layer; somatostatin and neuropeptide Y onto perforant terminals.

• STATE: membrane voltage, facilitation, recent spikes, fast conductance, peptide pool, calcium load.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 62 millivolts; threshold minus 42; 200 to 400 megaohms; facilitation ceiling five; burst four spikes in 50 milliseconds.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 25 to 40 milliseconds; facilitation 200 to 300; conductance 20 to 30; peptide seconds.

• FAILURE MODE: HIPP and mossy cells are the most vulnerable hilar populations in temporal lobe epilepsy, and their loss defines hippocampal sclerosis.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to recruit a HIPP cell from converging granule input and release peptide only on a real burst.
Create a number variable called voltage with initial value -62.
Create a number variable called facilitation with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called peptide with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called burst_count with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called index with initial value 0.
While index is less than the total number of items in granule_counts, repeat the following steps.
    Set active to the item at position index in granule_counts.
    Set drive to the product of 0.3 and the product of facilitation and active.
    Set voltage to the sum of voltage and drive.
    Set facilitation to the sum of facilitation and the product of 0.5 and active.
    If voltage is greater than or equal to -42, then perform the following actions.
        Set voltage to -67.
        Set burst_count to the sum of burst_count and 1.
    End the conditional block.
    If burst_count is greater than or equal to 4, then perform the following actions.
        Set peptide to the sum of peptide and 0.2.
        Set burst_count to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Set index to the sum of index and 1.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

Python, because the point is the contrast between scattered and converging input, shown by one function on two inputs.

import math

def hipp(counts):
    v, f, pep, recent, spikes = -62.0, 1.0, 0.0, [], 0
    for t, n in enumerate(counts):
        v += (-62.0 - v) / 30.0 + 0.30 * f * n   # facilitating collateral drive
        f = min(5.0, f + 0.5 * n) + (1.0 - f) / 250.0
        if v >= -42.0:
            v, spikes = -67.0, spikes + 1
            recent.append(t)
        recent = [s for s in recent if t - s <= 50]
        if len(recent) >= 4:                     # only a burst frees peptide
            pep, recent = pep + 0.2, []
        pep *= math.exp(-1 / 3000.0)
    return spikes, round(pep, 4)

print("scattered:", hipp([1 if t % 20 == 0 else 0 for t in range(1000)]))
print("converging:", hipp([12 if 200 <= t < 400 and t % 4 == 0 else 0
                           for t in range(1000)]))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Thorny dendrites that never leave the hilus, an axon filling the outer molecular layer and stopping at a sharp line, somatostatin with neuropeptide Y, and a grim signature, because in hippocampal sclerosis the hilus is the empty room this cell used to occupy.

CHAPTER 54. HILAR COMMISSURAL-ASSOCIATIONAL PATHWAY-RELATED (HICAP) CELLS (Local-Input Curbing Neuron) (COG)

This is the cell cannabis acts on in the dentate gyrus, much of why the drug blocks new memories.

Local-Input names the band this cell governs - the inner third of the molecular layer - where the dentate's own internal and cross-hemisphere fibres terminate rather than the cortical ones.

Curbing names the action - a checking of that internal recurrent drive so the dentate does not run away on its own echo.

The word also separates it cleanly from its neighbour that watches the outer band, and it records a crucial wiring fact: these cholecystokinin-containing cells inhibit basket cells as well as granule cells, so their restraint is aimed partly at other inhibitors.

Neuron marks it as a local inhibitory circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The hilar commissural-associational pathway-related cell, the HICAP cell, is the HIPP cell's counterpart aimed at a different layer.

It targets the inner third of the molecular layer, where fibres arrive from mossy cells of both hippocampi, setting how loudly the hippocampus talks to its front door.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The body lies in the hilus near the granule cell layer, and unlike the HIPP cell its dendrites cross that layer, while its axon fills only the inner third.

SIZE:

The body is 15 to 25 micrometres, dendrites reach 300 to 600 micrometres across two layers, and the axon field spans a few hundred.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Its closest relative is the total molecular layer cell, whose axon spans inner to outer, and both count as accommodating cannabinoid-sensitive cells.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The hilus of the dentate gyrus near the granule cell layer, with dendrites in hilus and molecular layer and an axon in the inner molecular layer.

The defining interface is the invisible line between inner third and outer two thirds, dividing memory-internal from world-external information.

NUMBER:

No human count exists and rodent figures are soft, but cholecystokinin cells are roughly 14 percent of interneurons in the quantified CA1 field.

A defensible estimate is 50,000 to 100,000 per hemisphere, 100,000 to 200,000 in both, about 0.1 percent of hippocampal formation neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Accommodating and regular spiking, intervals lengthening during maintained input, with 150 to 350 megaohms, a 15 to 30 millisecond time constant and broad spikes.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Excited by granule cell collaterals and by incoming pathways through its molecular layer dendrites, it inhibits granule cell proximal dendrites strongly, interneurons weakly.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Thin, unmyelinated and moderately branched, with terminals studded with cannabinoid receptors, and an output smeared in time after its spike.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the quietening chemical, released synchronously and asynchronously, with cholecystokinin as a slow modulator linked to anxiety.

MYELINATION:

None, and pointless here, because output timing is set by loosely coupled release machinery, not conduction speed.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Moderate and unremarkable, the distinctive cost being that asynchronous release empties vesicle pools more completely, so terminals refill constantly.

PLASTICITY:

Its plasticity runs backwards, because a depolarised granule cell makes endocannabinoids that cross the synapse and mute these terminals.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Nothing dramatic distinguishes the glia here, worth saying plainly, except that astrocytes carry cannabinoid receptors and read the signal.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove it and the dentate gyrus loses control of its own feedback, so the loop from granule cells to mossy cells and back gains dangerous gain.

It also loses the cannabinoid switch that frees an active granule cell.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Retrograde messaging is the headline, the target instructing its own inhibitor second by second.

Asynchronous release gives the terminal a chemical memory, spilling GABA past the spike.

Endocannabinoids also modulate electrical coupling here, so one signal adjusts two channels at once.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: granule cell collaterals; commissural and perforant excitation onto molecular layer dendrites; retrograde endocannabinoids.

• OUTPUTS: synchronous and asynchronous GABA onto granule cell proximal dendrites; cholecystokinin.

• STATE: membrane voltage, adaptation, terminal calcium, endocannabinoid suppression, vesicle pool.

• PARAMETERS: rest minus 60 to minus 68 millivolts; 150 to 350 megaohms; release probability 0.3 to 0.4, falling to zero under suppression.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 15 to 30 milliseconds; adaptation 100 to 300; calcium 50 to 100; suppression 2 to 10 seconds.

• FAILURE MODE: cannabinoids suppress these terminals and impair encoding, and in epilepsy models mutual inhibition among these cells is strengthened.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to release transmitter from a HICAP terminal, combining unreliable synchronous release, trailing asynchronous release, and endocannabinoid suppression.
Create a number variable called calcium with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called endocannabinoid with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called released with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called index with initial value 0.
While index is less than the total number of items in spike_trace, repeat the following steps.
    Set endocannabinoid to the sum of endocannabinoid and the item at position index in depolarisation_trace.
    Set suppression to the quotient of 1 divided by the sum of 1 and endocannabinoid.
    If the item at position index in spike_trace is equal to 1, then perform the following actions.
        Set calcium to the sum of calcium and 1.4.
        If random_draw is less than the product of 0.35 and suppression, then perform the following actions.
            Set released to the sum of released and 1.
        End the conditional block.
    End the conditional block.
    Set trailing to the product of 0.02 and the product of calcium and suppression.
    If random_draw is less than trailing, then perform the following actions.
        Set released to the sum of released and 1.
    End the conditional block.
    Set calcium to the product of calcium and 0.9835.
    Set endocannabinoid to the product of endocannabinoid and 0.99975.
    Set index to the sum of index and 1.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

JavaScript is chosen because the behaviour is stochastic and comparative, and Node runs it with no installation.

// HICAP terminal: unreliable synchronous release, trailing asynchronous release,
// and retrograde endocannabinoid suppression from the granule cell.
function run(spikeTimes, depolTime, steps) {
  let pool = 0, ecb = 0, released = 0;
  const spikes = new Set(spikeTimes);
  for (let t = 0; t < steps; t++) {
    if (t === depolTime) ecb += 1.0;   // granule cell makes endocannabinoid
    const supp = 1 / (1 + ecb);        // CB1 receptors mute the terminal
    if (spikes.has(t)) {
      if (Math.random() < 0.35 * supp) released += 1;   // synchronous
      pool += 1.4;                                      // residual calcium
    }
    if (Math.random() < 0.02 * pool * supp) released += 1;  // asynchronous
    pool *= Math.exp(-1 / 60.0);
    ecb *= Math.exp(-1 / 4000.0);
  }
  return released;
}
const burst = [100, 110, 120, 130, 140, 150, 160, 170];
let free = 0, muted = 0;
for (let i = 0; i < 200; i++) { free += run(burst, -1, 800); muted += run(burst, 50, 800); }
console.log("free:", (free / 200).toFixed(2), "suppressed:", (muted / 200).toFixed(2));

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A hilar cell whose dendrites cross the granule cell layer while its axon stops at the inner third, cholecystokinin in the body with cannabinoid receptors on the terminals, and a signature no other dentate cell shows, falling silent for seconds because the cell it inhibits told it to.

CHAPTER 55. MOLECULAR LAYER PERFORANT PATH-ASSOCIATED (MOPP) CELLS (Incoming-Path Pre-Emptive Neuron) (COG)

There is a cell in your brain whose only job is to say "not yet" to the memory system, and it says it about four milliseconds before the memory system has finished hearing the question.

Incoming-Path names what excites it - the cortical fibres entering the dentate - which it listens to directly instead of waiting for granule cells.

Pre-Emptive names the timing - and it is the measured fact that earns the name, because when that path is stimulated, these cells fire before the granule cells do, and their activation alone produces strong inhibition of granule cells.

The word also marks its role as a feed-forward brake applied to a message while it is still arriving, making it the natural counterpart of the feedback cell that answers only after the dentate has spoken.

Neuron marks it as a local inhibitory circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The molecular layer perforant path-associated cell, almost always shortened to MOPP cell, is an inhibitory nerve cell in the dentate gyrus, the entry gate of the hippocampus.

Inhibitory means it releases gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's main "quieten down" chemical messenger.

Its name is a description of where it sits and what it listens to.

Its cell body sits in the molecular layer, the thin outer rind of the dentate gyrus.

Its output wire spreads through exactly the band where the perforant path ends, the perforant path being the great cable of axons from the entorhinal cortex that "perforates" the nearby subiculum on its way into the hippocampus.

The MOPP cell is therefore wired in parallel with the granule cells, the dentate's principal output neurons, and it receives the same incoming message they do.

Because it is small and electrically excitable, it reaches firing threshold first.

The result is feed-forward inhibition: the news from the outside world arrives, the MOPP cell fires within a few milliseconds, and the granule cells find their dendrites already being silenced by the time the excitation builds.

This narrows the window in which a granule cell may fire to a few milliseconds, and that narrow window is one of the physical reasons the dentate gyrus fires so sparsely.

Sparse firing is the machinery of pattern separation, the trick by which two similar mornings become two separable memories.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The cell body is multipolar or triangular, sitting inside the molecular layer rather than in the granule cell layer beneath it.

Its dendrites, the input branches, stay inside the molecular layer and run flat along it, which means the cell samples entorhinal input and almost nothing else.

The dendrites are smooth or only sparsely studded with spines.

The axon, the output wire, forms a dense horizontal plexus confined to the middle and outer two-thirds of the molecular layer, avoiding the inner third where the commissural and associational fibres from mossy cells terminate.

That avoidance is the signature: this cell inhibits only the pathway it listens to.

SIZE:

The cell body is roughly 10 to 18 micrometres across, about one fifth the width of a human hair.

The dendritic field spans 300 to 600 micrometres along the molecular layer.

Reconstructed axons in rodents run for several millimetres to a few tens of millimetres in total length and carry on the order of one thousand to ten thousand release sites.

Human values are extrapolated from rodent reconstructions and should be treated as estimates.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Classical wide-field MOPP cells, with a long horizontal axon sweeping across the molecular layer.

• Neurogliaform-like MOPP cells, whose axon is a compact dense web and which release GABA largely by spillover into the tissue rather than into tidy synapses.

• Total molecular layer (TML) cells, a neighbouring type whose axon covers all three sublayers rather than sparing the inner third, and which are sensitive to cannabinoid signalling.

• Outer-molecular-layer-only variants, aligned to the lateral perforant path alone.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Dentate gyrus, stratum moleculare (the molecular layer), in both the suprapyramidal and infrapyramidal blades and in the crest that joins them.

It is present along the whole septotemporal axis, meaning from the dorsal hippocampus, which is more concerned with space, to the ventral hippocampus, which is more concerned with emotion.

The critical interface is the hippocampal fissure, the seam where the dentate molecular layer presses against the stratum lacunosum-moleculare of CA1, and MOPP dendrites and axons run right up to that seam.

NUMBER:

The five types in this batch are MOPP cells, trilaminar cells, back-projection cells, hippocampo-septal projecting inhibitory cells and radiatum-retrohippocampal cells, and together they are allocated about 360,000 neurons across both hemispheres.

That is roughly 0.24 percent of the 150 million neuron budget for the hippocampal formation, and about 2.4 percent of the roughly 15 million hippocampal interneurons in that budget.

MOPP cells take the largest share of the five: about 60,000 per hemisphere, so about 120,000 across both hemispheres, or 0.08 percent of the hippocampal formation.

Confidence is low.

The figure is derived by scaling: GABAergic cells are about 2 to 3 percent of dentate neurons, and molecular layer types are a minority of those.

No unbiased stereological count of human MOPP cells exists, so treat 30,000 to 120,000 per hemisphere as the defensible bound.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting membrane potential is around minus 60 to minus 65 millivolts.

Input resistance is high, roughly 150 to 400 megaohms, which means small currents move this cell a long way.

The membrane time constant, the time the cell takes to respond to a steady input, is about 10 to 25 milliseconds.

Action potentials are relatively broad for an interneuron, with a half-width of about 0.6 to 1.2 milliseconds, so this is not a fast-spiking cell.

Firing is regular or accommodating, sometimes late-spiking in the neurogliaform-like subset, with maximum sustained rates of roughly 20 to 60 hertz.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs come overwhelmingly from the perforant path axons of entorhinal cortex layer two, acting through AMPA and NMDA glutamate receptors.

Additional input arrives from other interneurons, from GABAergic and cholinergic fibres of the medial septum, and from serotonin and noradrenaline fibres of the brainstem.

Outputs land on the distal dendritic shafts and spines of granule cells, on newly born granule cells generated by adult neurogenesis, on mossy cell distal dendrites, and on other interneurons.

A single MOPP cell can contact several thousand granule cells, because its axon runs across the layer rather than down through it.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

The axon is thin, roughly 0.2 to 0.5 micrometres in diameter, and conducts slowly at about 0.1 to 0.3 metres per second.

It stays inside the dentate molecular layer, occasionally crossing the hippocampal fissure into CA1 stratum lacunosum-moleculare.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

GABA is the transmitter.

Many MOPP cells also carry neuropeptide Y (NPY), a peptide that suppresses glutamate release, and neuronal nitric oxide synthase (nNOS), the enzyme that makes the gas nitric oxide.

Reelin and the transcription factor COUP-TFII are common in the neurogliaform-like members.

These cells are typically negative for parvalbumin and for somatostatin.

Receptors on the cell include GABA-A, GABA-B, nicotinic and muscarinic acetylcholine receptors, and mu-opioid receptors in the neurogliaform-like subset.

MYELINATION:

Essentially none.

Its axon is bare, which is one reason it is slow and one reason it is cheap to run.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Modest by interneuron standards, because it does not fire at hundreds of hertz.

Mitochondrial density and cytochrome oxidase activity are lower than in fast-spiking basket cells, and these cells rarely wear the dense sugar-and-protein jackets called perineuronal nets.

Their steady, low-rate work still makes them continuously active consumers rather than occasional ones.

PLASTICITY:

The perforant path synapse onto MOPP cells can strengthen and weaken with use, which retunes how much feed-forward braking a given input produces.

MOPP cells innervate newborn granule cells, and because GABA is depolarising in very immature neurons, MOPP output helps steer the maturation and wiring of cells born in adulthood.

In the total molecular layer relatives, GABA release is suppressed by endocannabinoids acting on CB1 receptors, giving granule cells a retrograde volume control over their own inhibition.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The astrocytes of the dentate molecular layer are not generic.

They face the heaviest glutamate flux in the dentate, and they carry unusually high densities of the glutamate transporter EAAT2 to mop it up.

More specifically for this cell, MOPP and neurogliaform-like cells release GABA into the extracellular space rather than only into synaptic clefts, so the astrocytic GABA transporter GAT-3 in this layer directly sets how far and how long that inhibition spreads.

Astrocyte processes here also wrap the amyloid-prone perforant path terminal zone, which is why this layer is an early site of plaque deposition.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete MOPP cells and the dentate gyrus loses its first refusal.

The entorhinal barrage would reach granule cell dendrites unopposed for the first several milliseconds, widening the firing window from a few milliseconds to tens of milliseconds.

More granule cells would fire per input, the code would stop being sparse, and pattern separation would blur, so similar experiences would begin to overwrite one another.

The layer would also lose its slow, lingering, tissue-wide GABA tone, and gain a jumpy, all-or-nothing quality.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

This cell is a volume broadcaster.

GABA that escapes its release sites activates GABA-B receptors and extrasynaptic GABA-A receptors containing the delta subunit, producing inhibition that lasts hundreds of milliseconds rather than tens.

Neurogliaform-type MOPP cells are electrically coupled to one another and to other interneuron types through gap junctions, small protein pores that let current pass directly between cells, so they act partly as a syncytium.

Nitric oxide made by nNOS diffuses freely, acts backwards onto presynaptic terminals, and dilates local capillaries, which means this cell helps decide where blood goes in the molecular layer.

Neuropeptide Y released from its terminals suppresses glutamate release from the perforant path and is powerfully anticonvulsant.

Because its dendrites sit in a single lamina, it is close to a linear integrator of entorhinal drive rather than a dendritic computer, which is precisely what a timing device should be.

Its release is markedly asynchronous, with vesicles continuing to fuse for tens of milliseconds after the spike.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Perforant path glutamatergic drive from entorhinal cortex layer two, modelled as conductance in the range 0 to 20 nanosiemens, with AMPA rise 0.5 milliseconds and NMDA voltage dependence.

◦ Septal GABAergic and cholinergic modulation, 0 to 1 normalised tone.

◦ Local interneuron GABA input, 0 to 5 nanosiemens.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Fast synaptic GABA-A conductance onto granule cell middle and outer dendrites, about 0.5 to 2 nanosiemens per spike.

◦ Slow extrasynaptic GABA-B and delta-subunit GABA-A conductance from spillover, about 0.1 to 0.4 nanosiemens per spike.

◦ Nitric oxide and neuropeptide Y as diffusible scalar fields over a radius of roughly 20 to 100 micrometres.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane potential, refractory timer, fast inhibitory conductance, slow spillover conductance, NMDA gating variable, peptide store fraction.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Input resistance 150 to 400 megaohms, threshold about minus 45 millivolts, resting potential minus 65 millivolts, spike half-width 0.6 to 1.2 milliseconds, divergence 1,000 to 5,000 granule cells.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Membrane 10 to 25 milliseconds, GABA-A decay 5 to 12 milliseconds, spillover GABA-B 150 to 300 milliseconds, refractory 2 to 3 milliseconds, feed-forward latency 3 to 6 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ In temporal lobe epilepsy the loss of dentate inhibition removes the feed-forward brake and lets the perforant path drive granule cells directly into synchronous discharge.

◦ In Alzheimer's disease the perforant path terminal zone in this exact layer is an early site of amyloid deposition and synapse loss, so the MOPP cell's input is degraded before its own machinery fails, and the dentate's sparse code degrades early.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compute the feed-forward inhibition that a molecular layer perforant path-associated cell places onto dentate granule cells.
Create a number variable called membrane_voltage with initial value -65.
Create a number variable called fast_inhibition with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called slow_inhibition with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called refractory_timer with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called step_count with initial value 0.
Create a list variable called spike_steps with initial value empty list.
While step_count is less than 2000, repeat the following steps.
    Set perforant_drive to the entorhinal glutamate input for this step.
    Check if refractory_timer is greater than 0.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the difference of refractory_timer and 1.
        Set refractory_timer to that result.
    Otherwise, perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of perforant_drive and 0.02.
        Calculate the sum of membrane_voltage and that product.
        Set membrane_voltage to that result.
        Check if membrane_voltage is greater than or equal to -45.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set membrane_voltage to -65.
            Set refractory_timer to 25.
            Add step_count to the end of spike_steps.
            Calculate the sum of fast_inhibition and 1.2.
            Set fast_inhibition to that result.
            Calculate the sum of slow_inhibition and 0.25.
            Set slow_inhibition to that result.
        End the conditional block.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the product of fast_inhibition and 0.9876.
    Set fast_inhibition to that result.
    Calculate the product of slow_inhibition and 0.9994.
    Set slow_inhibition to that result.
    Calculate the sum of step_count and 1.
    Set step_count to that result.
End the loop.
Get the total number of items in spike_steps.
Display that total to the console.
Display slow_inhibition to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: multi-timescale synaptic kinetics are clearest as a few plain exponentials.
DT = 0.1  # milliseconds per step
class MoppFeedForward:
    """Perforant path -> MOPP cell -> granule cell distal dendrite."""
    def __init__(self):
        self.v = -65.0        # MOPP membrane potential, millivolts
        self.tau_m = 15.0     # membrane time constant, milliseconds
        self.r_in = 250.0     # input resistance, megaohms
        self.thresh = -45.0   # spike threshold, millivolts
        self.refrac = 0.0
        self.g_a = 0.0        # fast synaptic GABA-A conductance, nanosiemens
        self.g_b = 0.0        # slow spillover GABA-B conductance, nanosiemens
        self.tau_a = 8.0      # milliseconds
        self.tau_b = 180.0    # milliseconds, volume transmission outlasts the spike
    def step(self, i_pp):
        """i_pp is perforant path drive in picoamps."""
        fired = False
        if self.refrac > 0.0:
            self.refrac -= DT
        else:
            # r_in (megaohm) * current (picoamp) * 1e-3 gives millivolts.
            self.v += (-(self.v + 65.0) + self.r_in * i_pp * 1e-3) / self.tau_m * DT
            if self.v >= self.thresh:
                self.v = -65.0
                self.refrac = 2.5
                self.g_a += 1.20   # wired synapse onto the granule cell dendrite
                self.g_b += 0.25   # GABA that escapes the cleft and pools
                fired = True
        self.g_a -= self.g_a / self.tau_a * DT
        self.g_b -= self.g_b / self.tau_b * DT
        return fired
cell = MoppFeedForward()
n, first = 0, None
for k in range(2000):                                  # 200 milliseconds
    t = k * DT
    drive = 300.0 if 20.0 <= t < 60.0 else 0.0         # 40 ms perforant path burst
    if cell.step(drive):
        n += 1
        if first is None:
            first = t - 20.0
print("MOPP spikes in the burst:", n)
print("latency to first spike: %.1f ms" % first)
print("slow inhibition still present at 200 ms: %.3f nS" % cell.g_b)
# Output: 5 spikes, 4.6 ms latency, 0.512 nS of slow inhibition still running.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A GABA cell whose body sits inside the dentate molecular layer and whose axon fills the outer two-thirds of that layer while deliberately skipping the inner third.

No other dentate interneuron aligns itself so exactly to the perforant path and to nothing else.

CHAPTER 56. TRILAMINAR CELLS (Three-Layer Handover Neuron) (COG)

This cell fires more than twenty times a second while you are in dreamless sleep, in bursts faster than two hundred hertz, and then falls almost silent the moment you start to dream.


What is solidly established here is anatomy rather than behaviour, so the name records only that.

Three-Layer names the unusually wide local axon - which spreads through three separate layers of area CA1 instead of respecting a single band as most inhibitory cells do.

Handover names the second half of the same axon - which leaves the hippocampus altogether and innervates the subiculum, the region that carries hippocampal output onward.

The cell is marked by a high level of the $M_2$ acetylcholine receptor on its body and dendrites and by incoming terminals rich in a group III metabotropic glutamate receptor, but no single agreed behavioural role has yet been shown, and the name deliberately claims none.

Neuron marks it as a local inhibitory circuit cell.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

The trilaminar cell is an inhibitory GABA-releasing neuron of the hippocampal CA1 region, named because its output wire fans out through exactly three of CA1's layers.

It is also a long-range projection cell, which is unusual: most inhibitory cells whisper only to their immediate neighbours, but this one sends a branch out of CA1 entirely, into the subiculum, the hippocampus's main output station.

Its most surprising property is what it inhibits.

Between 59 and 84 percent of its identified output targets are the dendrites of other inhibitory neurons rather than excitatory pyramidal cells, and 37 percent of those inhibitory targets are parvalbumin-expressing cells, the fast basket cells that set hippocampal rhythm.

Inhibiting an inhibitor releases the brake, so the trilaminar cell is largely a disinhibitor: it hands control of CA1 back to the pyramidal cells at moments of its own choosing.

Because it also broadcasts to the subiculum, it tells the hippocampus's exit gate what state CA1 is in.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The cell body sits in stratum oriens, the layer beneath the CA1 pyramidal cell bodies, right up against the alveus, the white matter sheet that carries axons out of the hippocampus.

Its dendrites run horizontally and stay inside stratum oriens.

That single detail dictates its logic: stratum oriens is where CA1 pyramidal cells send their own local axon collaterals, so the trilaminar cell listens almost exclusively to CA1's own output and is therefore a feedback device.

The axon is very large.

One fully reconstructed cell had about 62 millimetres of axon, with the heaviest branching in stratum oriens and the alveus, less in stratum pyramidale and stratum radiatum, and none at all in stratum lacunosum-moleculare, plus a long branch running to the subiculum.

The same cell had 8.3 millimetres of dendrite, all in one layer.

SIZE:

The cell body is roughly 15 to 25 micrometres along its long axis and flattened horizontally.

Total dendritic length is about 8 millimetres; total axonal length about 62 millimetres, which is longer than a credit card is wide, packed into a few cubic millimetres of tissue plus one long projection.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• CA1 trilaminar cells projecting to the subiculum, the best characterised form.

• CA3 trilaminar cells, with the same three-layer local pattern in CA3.

• Trilaminar cells projecting to other cortical targets in addition to the subiculum.

• A defining negative subtype boundary: trilaminar cells do not project to the medial septum, which separates them from the hippocampo-septal cells described later in this section.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Hippocampus CA1, stratum oriens, concentrated at the oriens and alveus border.

Also present in CA3 stratum oriens.

There are two interfaces that matter.

The first is the oriens and alveus border, where grey matter meets the white matter sheet, and where this cell places its soma.

The second is the CA1 and subiculum junction, which its long axon crosses.

NUMBER:

Trilaminar cells are allocated about 25,000 per hemisphere, so about 50,000 across both hemispheres, which is roughly 0.03 percent of the 150 million neuron budget for the hippocampal formation.

Confidence is very low.

The estimate is built from rarity in intracellular labelling studies, where trilaminar cells appear as single-digit counts among dozens of recovered CA1 interneurons, and from the fact that they are negative for somatostatin and parvalbumin, so they lie outside the three human interneuron populations that have been counted stereologically.

A defensible bound is 10,000 to 60,000 per hemisphere.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

In the freely moving rat this cell is a high-rate, bursting neuron whose rate is set by brain state.

Mean rates are about 21.6 hertz in slow wave sleep, 19 hertz in quiet wakefulness, 8.5 hertz during movement, 5 hertz across theta epochs and 1.8 hertz in rapid eye movement sleep.

Within bursts it exceeds 200 hertz, and bursting is about 2.5 times more frequent in slow wave sleep than during movement.

Intervals between spikes are consistently under 9 milliseconds, with medians between 3.5 and 8.9 milliseconds depending on state.

During theta, the roughly 8 hertz rhythm of active exploration, it fires on the ascending slope at a mean phase of about 84 degrees, which is almost exactly 180 degrees away from the somatostatin-positive projection cells.

Detailed in vitro measurements of input resistance and time constant for identified trilaminar cells are scarce, and that gap should be stated rather than filled with invented numbers.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Inputs are dominated by local CA1 pyramidal cell axon collaterals in stratum oriens.

It also receives a specific and unusual GABAergic input from the medial septum, with about 12 septal terminals counted per cell section, of which about 29 percent carry the metabotropic glutamate receptor mGluR8a.

About 89 percent of the mGluR8a-positive terminals contacting these cells are GABAergic, and 66 percent of those also carry mGluR7a.

Outputs go chiefly to interneuron dendrites in CA1, including parvalbumin basket cells, with a minority onto pyramidal cell dendrites, plus the long-range projection into the subiculum.

Its output synapses have specialised postsynaptic densities, meaning the receiving side is structurally distinctive.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Local collaterals are thin and unmyelinated, conducting at a few tenths of a metre per second.

The subicular branch runs in the alveus and stratum oriens, is at least partly myelinated, and conducts considerably faster, on the order of 1 to 2 metres per second.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

GABA.

The defining chemical fingerprint is a very high level of the M2 muscarinic acetylcholine receptor on the cell body and dendrites, encoded by the gene Chrm2, combined with an absence of somatostatin and of parvalbumin.

The M2 receptor is inhibitory, coupled to the Gi class of signalling proteins, so acetylcholine turns this cell down rather than up.

MYELINATION:

Partial.

The long-range branch acquires myelin as it travels in the alveus toward the subiculum, while the local three-layer plexus stays bare.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

High.

Sixty-two millimetres of axon and sustained firing near 20 hertz with 200 hertz bursts is expensive, and the cost is dominated by the sodium and potassium pumping needed along that axon.

PLASTICITY:

The septal input is under presynaptic metabotropic control, because group three glutamate receptors mGluR8a and mGluR7a sitting on those GABAergic terminals let ambient glutamate reduce GABA release onto this cell, a form of cross-talk between two transmitter systems.

Cholinergic modulation through M2 is the main dynamic gain control, and it neatly explains the firing data: acetylcholine is high during movement and rapid eye movement sleep, exactly the states in which this cell goes quiet.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Oligodendrocytes of the alveus myelinate the subicular branch, and that is the one clearly specialised piece of glial support this cell receives.

Otherwise its astrocytic and microglial support in stratum oriens appears unremarkable compared with neighbouring interneurons.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove the trilaminar cell and CA1 loses its sleep-time disinhibition switch.

During slow wave sleep, when memories are replayed and consolidated, the parvalbumin basket cells would no longer be intermittently released from control at 200 hertz burst timing.

The subiculum would also stop receiving a direct, fast, inhibitory bulletin about CA1's state, so the hippocampus's exit gate would have to infer that state from excitation alone.

The region would keep its rhythms but lose a layer of state-dependent gating that switches sign between sleep and movement.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The trilaminar cell is a state broadcaster more than a computer.

Its dendrites live in one layer, so it performs almost no dendritic computation, and instead sums CA1's own output faithfully.

Its interesting machinery is at its terminals and on its membrane: Gi-coupled M2 receptors that translate cholinergic state into firing rate, presynaptic group three metabotropic glutamate receptors on its septal inputs that allow heterosynaptic control, specialised postsynaptic densities at its outputs, and a burst code above 200 hertz that is likely read by target interneurons as a distinct symbol from single spikes.

Its long myelinated branch means it also participates in the metabolic and signalling dialogue with oligodendrocytes that adjusts conduction delay.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Local CA1 pyramidal collateral glutamate, 0 to 30 nanosiemens, feedback only.

◦ Medial septal GABA input, about 12 terminals per section, with 29 percent mGluR8a positive, modelled as a 0 to 5 nanosiemens inhibitory conductance under presynaptic metabotropic scaling.

◦ Cholinergic tone from the medial septum as a scalar 0 to 1, acting through Gi-coupled M2 receptors.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ GABA-A conductance onto CA1 interneuron dendrites, 59 to 84 percent of targets, of which 37 percent are parvalbumin cells.

◦ GABA-A conductance onto CA1 pyramidal dendrites in strata oriens, pyramidale and radiatum, never in lacunosum-moleculare.

◦ Long-range GABA output to the subiculum with a conduction delay of 1 to 4 milliseconds in rodent, scaled up in human.

• STATE.

◦ Membrane potential, burst counter, time since last spike, cholinergic suppression factor, presynaptic metabotropic scaling of septal input.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ M2 suppression gain about 0.9, burst probability about 0.35, spikes per burst about 3 to 4, intra-burst interval 3.5 to 9 milliseconds, axon length 62 millimetres, dendrite length 8.3 millimetres.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Intra-burst interval about 4 milliseconds, theta cycle 125 milliseconds with preferred phase 84 degrees, state transitions on the scale of seconds to minutes.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Cholinergic degeneration, as in Alzheimer's disease, removes the M2 brake and would leave this cell inappropriately active during movement, disinhibiting CA1 at the wrong times.

◦ Loss of the cell removes a targeted disinhibitory pathway onto parvalbumin cells, which is expected to flatten the sleep and wake difference in CA1 inhibitory tone and to degrade the sharp wave ripple replay that depends on it.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compute the state-dependent bursting output of a trilaminar cell under muscarinic M2 control.
Create a number variable called cholinergic_tone with initial value 0.05.
Create a number variable called excitatory_drive with initial value 10.8.
Create a number variable called m2_gain with initial value 0.9.
Create a number variable called burst_spikes_left with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called burst_countdown with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called spike_total with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called step_count with initial value 0.
Calculate the product of m2_gain and cholinergic_tone.
Calculate the difference of 1 and that product.
Calculate the product of excitatory_drive and that difference.
Set effective_rate to that result.
While step_count is less than 60000, repeat the following steps.
    Check if burst_spikes_left is greater than 0.
    If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the difference of burst_countdown and 1.
        Set burst_countdown to that result.
        Check if burst_countdown is less than or equal to 0.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Calculate the sum of spike_total and 1.
            Set spike_total to that result.
            Calculate the difference of burst_spikes_left and 1.
            Set burst_spikes_left to that result.
            Set burst_countdown to 4.
        End the conditional block.
    Otherwise, perform the following actions.
        Set random_draw to a fresh random number between 0 and 1.
        Calculate the quotient of effective_rate divided by 1000.
        Check if random_draw is less than that quotient.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Calculate the sum of spike_total and 1.
            Set spike_total to that result.
            Check if cholinergic_tone is less than 0.5.
            If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
                Set burst_spikes_left to 3.
                Set burst_countdown to 4.
            End the conditional block.
        End the conditional block.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the sum of step_count and 1.
    Set step_count to that result.
End the loop.
Display spike_total to the console.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: this cell is really a small state machine, and Python keeps the states readable.
import random
random.seed(7)
DT = 1.0          # milliseconds per step
M2_GAIN = 0.90    # M2 receptors are Gi-coupled, so acetylcholine subtracts drive
BURST_P = 0.35    # chance that a spike opens a high-frequency burst
BURST_N = 3       # extra spikes inside a burst
BURST_ISI = 4     # milliseconds between spikes inside a burst (measured 3.5 to 8.9)
# "ach" is measured cholinergic tone, 0 to 1. "drive" is local pyramidal plus
# septal excitation, fitted so the M2 term reproduces the measured rates.
STATES = {
    "slow_wave_sleep": {"ach": 0.05, "drive": 10.8},
    "quiet_wake":      {"ach": 0.10, "drive": 10.0},
    "movement":        {"ach": 0.85, "drive": 36.0},
    "rem_sleep":       {"ach": 1.00, "drive": 18.0},
}
def simulate(state, seconds=60):
    ach = STATES[state]["ach"]
    rate = STATES[state]["drive"] * (1.0 - M2_GAIN * ach)   # events per second
    spikes, left, wait = 0, 0, 0
    for _ in range(int(seconds * 1000 / DT)):
        if left > 0:
            wait -= DT
            if wait <= 0:
                spikes += 1
                left -= 1
                wait = BURST_ISI
        elif random.random() < rate * DT / 1000.0:
            spikes += 1
            if ach < 0.5 and random.random() < BURST_P:      # bursts need low acetylcholine
                left, wait = BURST_N, BURST_ISI
    return spikes / float(seconds)
for name in ("slow_wave_sleep", "quiet_wake", "movement", "rem_sleep"):
    print("%-16s %5.1f Hz" % (name, simulate(name)))
# Output: 20.9, 18.0, 8.3, 1.8 Hz against measured 21.6, 19.0, 8.5, 1.8 Hz.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

An axon that occupies exactly three CA1 layers, pointedly skipping stratum lacunosum-moleculare, combined with a heavy coat of M2 muscarinic receptors, no somatostatin, no parvalbumin, and a branch running into the subiculum but never into the septum.

APPENDIX 1. THE THREE-STAGE PROVING SYSTEM OF PREVIOUSLY UNNAMED CONSTRUCTS (PUC)


A1.1 Why the Names Must Be Proved

Eleven times in this work, a construct has been marked with the tag (PUC), meaning Previously Unnamed Construct, and given a name.

That is a strong act, and it carries a burden.

To claim that a thing is real, is used, and yet has never been named is to make three assertions at once, and each of them can be wrong.

The thing might not be real.

Clustering algorithms produce clusters whether or not nature put a boundary there, and a great deal of what looks like a cell type is a cut through a continuum, drawn where the analyst set a parameter.

It might already have a name that the author overlooked.

This is the commonest and most embarrassing failure, and it is the reason the second stage below carries the heaviest burden of the three.

Or the proposed name might be a poor one that the field is right to reject.

A responsible naming, therefore, is not a christening but a proof.

This appendix supplies that proof system, and the appendices that follow it apply the system to each Previously Unnamed Construct named in this volume.

No construct earns its name by assertion.

Each must survive the three stages.

The naming is never a claim of discovery.

Every construct named in this work was already real, already relied upon, already published in parts.

What was missing was the word, and a science cannot reason efficiently about a thing it cannot name.

The proving system is the discipline that ensures a proposed word deserves to exist.


A1.2 The Three Stages

Every construct is evaluated against three stages, in order.

A construct that fails any stage does not earn its name.

STAGE 1: CONCEPTUAL PRECISION.

The construct must be demonstrably real and currently relied upon in the neuroscience literature.

It must be physically or functionally distinct from every adjacent named construct, not merely a paraphrase of something already named.

And it must be functionally necessary, playing a specific causal role whose existence is documented, rather than being a convenience of description.

In short: the thing must exist, must be distinct, and must matter.

Applied to a cell type rather than to a brain region, this stage is sharpened into three specific tests.

• The convergence test, for reality.

Do independent methods agree that this population exists?

A cluster that appears only in gene-expression data, and that no anatomist, physiologist or connectivity study has ever separated from its neighbours, fails.

A population that appears as a distinct cluster in sequencing, a distinct shape under a microscope, a distinct firing pattern under an electrode, and a distinct projection target in a tracing study has passed the strongest test the field can apply.

• The boundary test, for distinctness.

Which named cell type is this one most likely to be confused with, and what specifically separates them?

The answer must be stated, not assumed.

• The ablation test, for necessity.

If this population is silenced, deleted, or lost to disease, what specifically fails?

Evidence that a cell is merely active during a behaviour is not enough.

Something must break.

STAGE 2: IDENTIFICATION OF A GAP, AND PROOF OF NAMELESSNESS.

The construct must lack an accepted, widely used proper name in the existing literature.

The burden here is high, and it is placed on the person claiming the gap rather than on the reader.

Informal descriptive phrases, pedagogical metaphors, and passing usages in single papers do not count as proper names.

An established proper name is one used consistently across the literature, appearing in standard textbooks, understood without further definition by the relevant community.

If any such name exists, even an imperfect or contested one, the construct does not qualify, and it is cited as a named concept instead.

For cell types one further clarification is needed, and it is the clarification that makes this exercise possible.

A label is not a name.

None of the following counts as an accepted proper name, however consistently it is used.

• A gene symbol, or a combination of gene symbols, such as Sst Chodl or Lamp5 Lhx6.

A gene symbol names a gene, designates the cell by a correlate, and changes when a better marker is found.

• An alphanumeric cluster identifier from any atlas or taxonomy, such as subcluster 0417 or MLI2.

• An anatomical coordinate, such as layer 6b or lamina II inner.

• A description of the method by which the cell was found, or of how it looks on a plot.

• A phrase that varies from paper to paper, so that two papers about the same cell cannot be retrieved by the same search.

An eponym, meaning a scientist's surname, does count as a proper name, and disqualifies a candidate.

A long-established functional term that appears in textbooks and is understood without definition, such as silent nociceptor or intrinsic primary afferent neuron, also counts and also disqualifies.

Each construct must state, by name, the strongest counter-candidate term and precisely why that term does not clear the bar.

STAGE 3: PEDAGOGICAL CLARITY.

The proposed name must be self-explanatory to an informed reader, must accurately describe the construct's identity and function, and must be more useful for teaching, research and clinical application than the current unnamed state.

Names that are opaque, misleading, excessively long, or redundant with existing terminology do not pass.

The name must earn its place.

Every name proposed in this work obeys six rules.

• Plain English, with no Latin, no Greek, no eponym, no gene symbol, no acronym and no alphanumeric string.

• The name tells the story of the cell's purpose, function and faculty, so that a first-year student meeting the name alone learns something true.

• Two to five words, ending in the word neuron or cell.

• Dignified enough to print in a journal and plain enough for a curious teenager.

• Not redundant with, and not confusable with, any existing neuron name, nor with the other names proposed alongside it.

• Compound modifiers hyphenated, so that the reader parses the name correctly on first reading.


A1.3 The Additional Considerations: Ten Properties of a Sound Construct

The three stages are the minimum.

To hold each construct to the maximum, each is also examined against ten properties, drawn from the qualities that distinguish a genuine construct from a mere label.

These are stated once here and then reported briefly for each construct.

REALITY: the construct denotes something that physically or functionally exists, not a modelling artefact.

NECESSITY: the construct is required to reason about the domain; remove it and something true becomes unsayable.

CONCEPTUAL PRECISION AND SPECIFICITY: the construct picks out one thing exactly, with sharp boundaries, not a fuzzy region.

IDENTIFICATION OF A GAP AND PROOF OF NAMELESSNESS: there is a demonstrable hole in the field's vocabulary that this construct fills, and no accepted proper name already occupies it.

PEDAGOGICAL CLARITY: the name teaches; a student meeting it learns something true about the construct from the name alone.

DIRECTIONALITY: what the cell takes in, and where it sends its output, is definite and stated, not ambiguous.

HIERARCHICAL POSITION: the construct sits at a definite layer of the fifteen-layer framework, and its relation to the layers above and below it is specified.

Every construct named in this work sits at Layer 6, the Cellular Layer, and each entry states the Layer 8 microcircuit motif it participates in and the Layer 9 region or pathway it helps to build.

UNIVERSALITY WITHIN ITS CLASS: the construct is not a one-off; it is an instance of a recognisable class, and the class is populated.

THEORETICAL COMPLETENESS IN HIERARCHICAL MODELS: naming the construct closes a gap that a complete hierarchical model of the nervous system would otherwise leave open.

CLINICAL CONSEQUENCE: a person is different because of it, whether through its loss, its excess, or a drug in current use that acts upon it.

For brevity, where a property is fully satisfied and needs no elaboration, it is marked as satisfied; where it requires comment, the comment is given.

A construct for which a property genuinely does not apply is marked (NA), meaning Not Applicable, and is always accompanied by the reason for its non-applicability, so that a reader never mistakes a property that does not apply for one that was merely omitted.


A1.4 How These Eleven Were Selected

The eleven Previously Unnamed Constructs named in this work were not chosen.

They are what survived.

Two sweeps were run across the whole of neuroscience, the first across twenty territories and the second across eighteen more, and together they returned four hundred and sixty-three candidate cell types, which merged into two hundred and twenty-nine distinct constructs.

Forty-six of those were carried into the proving system.

Each of the forty-six was then attacked by two independent skeptics working separately, neither of whom saw the other's report.

The first was instructed to refute the claim of namelessness, to search the standard textbooks, the review literature, the Petilla terminology, the Cell Ontology and the major brain atlases, and to reject the construct if it found any qualifying proper name, or if it was merely uncertain.

The second was instructed to refute the claim of reality, distinctness and necessity, to look for clustering artefacts, arbitrary cuts through continua, single-laboratory findings that failed to replicate, and rebrandings of things already known, and to demand causal evidence rather than correlation.

A construct passed only if both skeptics tried and failed.

Eleven survived.

Thirty-five did not.

Twenty-one of the thirty-five failed because a proper name already existed and the proposer had not found it, which is the single most important practical lesson of the exercise: the hard part of claiming that something is unnamed is not the claiming, it is the looking.

The appendices that follow apply the three stages, and the ten additional considerations, to each Previously Unnamed Construct that appears in this volume.


APPENDIX 2. Far-Hushing Slow-Wave Neuron, proved. (From CHAPTER 24.)

WHAT IT NAMES.

This name belongs to a rare, large nerve cell scattered through the neocortex, the brain's folded outer sheet, and packed most densely in its deepest layer.

It is inhibitory, meaning it quiets other cells, using gamma-aminobutyric acid (GABA), the brain's main quieting messenger, plus nitric oxide gas, somatostatin and neuropeptide Y.

Every other quieting cell in the cortex speaks only to its neighbours, whereas this one grows long output cables, called axons, that cross millimetres of cortex and the midline into the matching patch opposite.

It listens to nearby excitatory cells and to the peptide substance P, then spreads a slow hush over wide stretches of cortex, tipping the sheet towards the slow electrical swells of deep sleep and pacing local blood flow.

WHAT THE LITERATURE CALLS IT NOW.

In mice it is "Sst Chodl" and in humans "Inh L3-6 SST NPY", gene-symbol barcodes, the second padded with a class abbreviation and a layer range.

It is also "Int1" and "Sst-MET-1", bare cluster identifiers; "type-I nNOS neuron", a protein symbol joined to an ordinal awarded for staining darkly under the method that found it; and "long-range GABAergic projection neuron", an unstable descriptive phrase.

STAGE 1, CONCEPTUAL PRECISION.

The cell is real: nitric-oxide-enzyme staining, retrograde tracing, single-cell sequencing in mouse and human, cross-species matching and three genetic driver lines converge on one population.

Tomioka and colleagues showed in 2005 that 91 percent of long-distance inhibitory cortical cells carried somatostatin, and Agmon and Barth, sceptics of fine transcriptomic splits, still keep this group as a robust supertype.

It is distinct from the Martinotti cell, which also carries somatostatin and shapes slow waves but keeps its axon inside its own patch, whereas this cell exports its axon to other areas and the opposite hemisphere.

It is necessary: when Turner and colleagues killed it in mouse cortex, delta-band power, the measure of how deep the slow rhythm runs, fell both awake (p = 0.011) and asleep (p = 0.022), and the usual post-stimulus dip in blood volume vanished (p = 0.0005).

Those animals slept as long as before, so what failed was the depth and cortex-wide co-ordination of the rhythm, not sleep itself.

STAGE 2, IDENTIFICATION OF A GAP, AND PROOF OF NAMELESSNESS.

What is named is the local family: basket, chandelier, Martinotti, double bouquet, bitufted, bipolar and neurogliaform cells.

What is not named is the one exception: the cortical quieting cell that projects between areas and hemispheres and sets the depth and cortex-wide coherence of slow waves.

The strongest counter-candidate, "type-I nNOS neuron", is thirty years old and cross-lab, but nNOS is a gene symbol, "type I" an ordinal from staining intensity, it is in none of the four reference textbooks, and it teaches nothing about the cell.

The nearby coined name "long-range inhibitory nNOS-expressing cell" names a hippocampal population its authors distinguish from this one.

The gap is genuine.

STAGE 3, PEDAGOGICAL CLARITY.

"Cortex-Wide" teaches the fact that makes the cell unique: its influence is not local but reaches the whole sheet, including the far hemisphere.

"Slow-Wave" teaches what it delivers: the large, slow electrical swells that sweep the cortex in deep sleep, whose size measures sleep depth.
