"Neuron" teaches that a single nerve cell does this, not a region and not a chemical.

The insight from the name alone is that inhibition is not only local: one rare cell carries the quieting signal far enough for distant regions to sleep in step.

That unlocks sleep depth as an actively imposed, cell-addressable state, relevant to shallow sleep in ageing and in genetic syndromes.

ADDITIONAL CONSIDERATIONS.

• Reality.

Satisfied, five independent methods and five laboratories converge on one population.

• Necessity.

Satisfied, ablation strips out delta power and vasomotion, and silencing rescues a disease phenotype.

• Conceptual precision and specificity.

Satisfied at supertype level, though the group holds two or three finer clusters.

• Identification of a gap and proof of namelessness.

Satisfied, every label in use is a barcode, cluster number, staining ordinal or shifting phrase, absent from Kandel, Purves, Squire and Shepherd.

• Pedagogical clarity.

Satisfied, three plain words carry reach, effect and cell.

• Directionality.

It takes excitation from nearby pyramidal cells and substance P signals of behavioural state, and sends GABA, nitric oxide and neuropeptide Y locally and along long axons to distant cortical areas and across the midline.

• Hierarchical position.

Layer 6, the Cellular Layer, of the fifteen-layer framework; at Layer 8 the long-range inhibitory broadcast motif; at Layer 9 the cortex-to-cortex inhibitory pathway and the neurovascular unit; the Layer 10 consequence is global sleep depth, with a Layer 12 consequence for reading functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI) signals.

• Universality within its class.

Satisfied, present in every cortical area sampled and in rodents, macaque and human.

• Theoretical completeness in hierarchical models.

Satisfied, models with only local inhibition lack any element able to synchronise distant areas.

• Clinical consequence.

In a mouse model of human 15q13.3 microdeletion syndrome this cell showed the largest gene-expression disturbance of any cortical type, and silencing it rescued the sleep phenotype; no drug yet targets it.

SOURCES FOR THIS CONSTRUCT.

• Turner and colleagues, Type-I nNOS neurons orchestrate cortical neural activity and vasomotion, eLife, 2025.

• Ruff and colleagues, Long-range inhibitory neurons mediate cortical neurovascular coupling, Cell Reports, 2024.

• Morairty and colleagues, A role for cortical nNOS/NK1 neurons in coupling homeostatic sleep drive to EEG slow wave activity, Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences, 2013.

• Tomioka and colleagues, Demonstration of long-range GABAergic connections distributed throughout the mouse neocortex, European Journal of Neuroscience, 2005.

• Agmon and Barth, A brief history of somatostatin interneuron taxonomy, Frontiers in Neural Circuits, 2024.


APPENDIX 3. Cortex-To-Cortex Linking Neuron, proved. (From CHAPTER 8.)

WHAT IT NAMES.

This is an excitatory neuron, a nerve cell that urges others to fire, using glutamate, the brain's commonest excitatory messenger.

Its body sits deep in layer six, the lowest sheet of the cerebral cortex, the brain's folded outer rind, in its side and underside districts.

Its axon, the long output fibre, branches enormously, reaching an average of fourteen target areas, among the highest for any cortical cell.

Those targets are nearby cortex and the matching area across the midline, and in mouse never the striatum, the hub selecting actions.

So it carries news between distant patches of cortex without touching the machinery of movement.

WHAT THE LITERATURE CALLS IT NOW.

The labels in use are "L6 IT Car3", "L5/6 IT CAR3", "CTX L6 Car3" and "Ncx-Car3", each welding a gene-symbol barcode, Car3 for carbonic anhydrase 3, onto a layer coordinate.

The atlas entry "CLA-EPd-CTX Car3 Glut" and the ontology entry "L6 intratelencephalic projecting Car3 glutamatergic neuron" are cluster identifiers.

In primates it carries different gene symbols again, "Exc L5-6 THEMIS SMYD1" in human and "Exc THEMIS NTNG2" in marmoset.

The one non-genetic label, "claustrum-like cortical (CLC) neurons", is an unstable descriptive phrase from one group.

STAGE 1, CONCEPTUAL PRECISION.

The cell is real because unrelated methods converge on it.

Single-nucleus ribonucleic acid sequencing (RNA sequencing), which reads which genes a cell uses, resolved it as a subclass, and gene mapping inside intact tissue found it in place, alone among cortical types in making no layered band.

Whole-brain tracing reconstructed ninety-nine axons singly, rat viral tracing recovered it without sequencing, and comparative sequencing found it in five primates.

It is most easily confused with the ordinary layer six intratelencephalic neuron, its neighbour, which also keeps its axon inside the forebrain.

One clean fact separates them: in mouse those neurons branch into the striatum and this cell never does.

It is necessary, as shown by deleting Nr4a2, the switch gene installing and maintaining its identity.

Adult deletion leaves mice hyperactive and short of anxiety, and a second laboratory found the cells misplaced, uncoupled from amygdala and cingulate cortex, the animals inattentive.

What fails is the restraint that keeps an animal cautious and attentive.

No experiment has yet silenced the cortical members alone.

STAGE 2, IDENTIFICATION OF A GAP, AND PROOF OF NAMELESSNESS.

What is named here is the neighbourhood, not the cell.

The claustrum has been named for two centuries, "claustral neuron" is established for its inner cells, and cortex has Martinotti and basket cells, but none names this cell.

What is not named is this population, the deep, lateral, striatum-avoiding, many-targeted excitatory cell running the claustrum's programme outside it.

The strongest counter-candidate is "claustrum-like cortical (CLC) neuron".

It fails because it describes rather than names, is defined at first use in its own abstract, and comes from one group keyed to a different gene, so two papers on the cell share no search term.

The gap is genuine.

STAGE 3, PEDAGOGICAL CLARITY.

"Cortex-Only" teaches the restriction, that its output stays inside the cortical sheet and never reaches the striatum, the hub other long-range cortical cells feed.

"Far-Linking" teaches its reach, joining many cortical territories far apart, including the mirror-image territory across the midline.

"Neuron" teaches that a single nerve cell does this, not a tract or a region.

The insight from the name alone is that the cortex keeps a cell for letting its distant parts talk, barred from the machinery of action.

That insight unlocks attention and impulse control, which collapse when this cell's identity switch is removed.

ADDITIONAL CONSIDERATIONS.

• Reality.

Satisfied, since sequencing, in-tissue mapping, axon reconstruction and tracing all recover it across seven species.

• Necessity.

Satisfied, since adult loss of its identity switch produces hyperactivity, lost anxiety and inattention in two laboratories.

• Conceptual precision and specificity.

Satisfied, since the absence of striatal branches separates it from every other long-range cortical cell.

• Identification of a gap and proof of namelessness.

Satisfied, since every label in use is a gene symbol, layer coordinate or cluster identifier.

• Pedagogical clarity.

Satisfied, since the name gives the restriction and the reach without gene, layer or acronym.

• Directionality.

It takes in signals from surrounding cortex and returns them to other cortex, near and across the midline, never the striatum.

• Hierarchical position.

It belongs to Layer 6, the Cellular Layer, of the fifteen-layer framework.

At Layer 8 it joins the long-range cortico-cortical association motif, including the callosal mirror-image link.

At Layer 9 it builds the lateral cortical association network.

The Layer 10 consequence is cortex-wide co-ordination of attention.

• Universality within its class.

Satisfied, since it appears in every lateral cortical area examined and in five primates.

• Theoretical completeness in hierarchical models.

Satisfied, since no other cortical cell reaches so widely while feeding nothing to the striatal loop.

• Clinical consequence.

Losing one working copy of Nr4a2, the gene specifying this cell, causes a human neurodevelopmental disorder with intellectual disability, epilepsy and language impairment, and no therapy targets it selectively.

SOURCES FOR THIS CONSTRUCT.

• Peng and colleagues, Morphological diversity of single neurons in molecularly defined cell types, Nature, 2021.

• Yao and colleagues, A high-resolution transcriptomic and spatial atlas of cell types in the whole mouse brain, Nature, 2023.

• Tao and colleagues, Nr4a2, a key factor controlling the development and functional maintenance of forebrain Car3 neurons, Neuroscience Bulletin, 2026.

• Yan and colleagues, Nurr1 orchestrates claustrum development and functionality, Advanced Science, 2025.

• Jorstad and colleagues, Comparative transcriptomics reveals human-specific cortical features, Science, 2023.

• Fang and colleagues, Similarities of neocortical deep layer Oprk1-positive neurons and claustrum neurons in connectivity and activity, iScience, 2025.
